| Names on the card | The before state's `names` gains `classes` and `customers`, only when the new lines name any, so older before states read as they did. `ToolDisplay`'s line columns gain `tags`: refs shown by name under what a line is booked to (its job, its class); a client never reads a bare id, the back office reads `#id`. // DECISION |
| `qbo.vendor.create` | Verb `create`, entity `vendor` (vendors' verbs become read and create). Input: display name, company name, email, phone, billing address, terms id. Refused when proposed and again before writing if a vendor (active or not) has the same name, compared with case and spacing set aside; every vendor is read (1,000 a page, at most 20 pages), not filtered by name. QuickBooks' own "duplicate name" (a customer or employee with the name) is the same refusal. Unknown or inactive terms are refused. The before state is `{existing: null, billingAddress, terms}`. Right before writing, a vendor with the name, company and email asked for is this action's own lost write: reported done with its id. Output: the new vendor's id for a later step. // DECISION |
| `qbo.purchaseorder.create` | Verb `create`, entity `purchase_order`, `amount` = `expectedTotal`. The rules of `qbo.bill.create`: lines add up to the total (decimal.js), the total is more than zero, the vendor is the company's, `requestid` from the action's key. Number optional; with one, an order from the same vendor with the same number (letters and digits) is refused when proposed and before writing, read live from the vendor's orders (orders aren't synced). Without one, QuickBooks' `requestid` alone keeps a retry from writing twice. We send the one active A/P account in the order's currency when there is exactly one. The memo is `PrivateNote`; ship-to is `ShipAddr`; the expected date is `DueDate`. The card says we don't send the order to the vendor. Output: id, number, total, status. // DECISION |
| Zero totals | decimal.js counts zero as positive, so create bill let a zero total through. Both creates now refuse a total that isn't more than zero. // DECISION |
| Summaries | A stored amount like `1125.0000` read `1125.` in summaries; it now reads `1125.00` (core template). // DECISION |
| Permissions | Manifest entities gain `account` (read), `class` (read) and `purchase_order` (read, create); `vendor` gains create. The matrix, grants, the catalog and publish-time `entity_off` know them from the manifest. Both writes wait for approval unless the connection's create is `auto`, the step is `not_required` and the agent acts (invariant 8). |
| Fake | Serves Class, Term and PurchaseOrder from new fixtures, creates vendors (unique names across vendors and customers) and purchase orders (open, total from the lines), and refuses lines whose class or customer the company doesn't have. |
| Not built | Linking a bill to the purchase order it receives, closing an order, sending it to the vendor, and reading the company's class-tracking setting (NOTES.md). |
| Files | `packages/connectors/src/qbo/{ingredients,purchase-order}.ts` (new), P-2 blocks in `tool-kit.ts`, `records.ts`, `display.ts`, `fake.ts`, `index.ts`, `tools.ts`, `bill-create.ts`; `packages/core/src/schemas/tool-display.ts` (`tags`), `packages/core/src/template/template.ts` (one line); `apps/web/src/inbox/{change.ts,ChangePanel.tsx}` (tags), `apps/web/src/runs/labels.ts`; fixtures; tests. |

### 1.65 Attachments both ways (P-3, M36; no migration)

Ingredients for agents the owner builds in the Agent lab, such as a purchasing agent that emails a
vendor a purchase order PDF and reads the quote PDF that comes back. Platform and connector pieces
only: no agent, template or workflow. Built on `claude/p3-attachments` against the fakes. Facts we
couldn't check are in each connector's `NOTES.md` (resend, gmail, outlook, twilio, whatsapp_meta).
Hot-spot edits are blocks marked `// P-3`.

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| What can go | `attachments: [{documentId}]` on `email.send`, `gmail.send`, `outlook.send`, `sms.send` and `whatsapp.send` (both providers): stored documents of the call's own client, PDFs and images only, at most five, each once (core's `OutboundAttachments`). The name and type come from the document record, never the input. // DECISION |
| Read for one send | The tool reads each file through `ctx.services.documents` (apps/api's `dbDocumentServices`, inside `withTenant` for the connection's client), so another client's id reads as unknown and is refused (`not_found`, the same answer as an unknown id). The bytes are read for that send only, and checked against the record's type (magic bytes): a mismatch is refused. Nothing is started or sent before the checks pass. // DECISION |
| Size per provider | The files' total, as stored: Resend 29 MB (40 MB after base64), Gmail 18 MB (25 MB a message after base64), Outlook 3 MB (Graph's 4 MB request; larger files need an upload session on a draft and `Mail.ReadWrite`, which we don't ask for), MMS 5 MB, WhatsApp one file (Twilio 16 MB; Meta images 5 MB, documents 100 MB, a caption 1024 characters). A refusal names the limit in plain words (`AttachmentRefusedError`). // DECISION |
| Gmail's MIME | `multipart/mixed` built in `gmail/mime.ts`: the text first, then each file `Content-Disposition: attachment`, everything base64 in 76-character lines, so the message stays 7-bit ASCII. The boundary starts `=_` (never in base64) and comes from the Message-ID. A plain ASCII name is quoted; any other as RFC 2231 `filename*=UTF-8''…` beside an RFC 2047 `name`. Quotes, backslashes and line breaks never reach a header; the type must be `type/subtype`. With files, the message goes as media to Gmail's upload URI (`message/rfc822`, up to 35 MB); without, as JSON `raw` as before. // DECISION |
| Outlook's shape | Graph's `fileAttachment` in `message.attachments` of `sendMail`: `@odata.type`, `name`, `contentType`, `contentBytes` (base64). // DECISION |
| Texts and WhatsApp | Twilio fetches each file from a 5-minute signed link (`MediaUrl`, repeated; the Worker's `/api/files/:token` or R2's presigned link, `SIGNED_URL_TTL_SECONDS`). Meta gets one file by `link` (a PDF as `document` with its file name, JPEG and PNG as `image`), the text as its caption: not Meta's media upload, since the connector's HTTP interface carries text bodies only (upload is the fallback if links prove unreliable). A WhatsApp template (outside the 24-hour window) carries no file, so that send is refused. Links are made in the tool for the one send and never stored. // DECISION |
| Approval and records | The before state lists the files by name, type and size (`attachments` on each send's before schema), so the approval card shows them and `external_action.before` keeps them; the input keeps the ids and the output the same list. The thread line adds "Attached: PO-1042.pdf (12 KB)". Card-number redaction, consent, quiet hours, once-only sends and approval are unchanged. // DECISION |
| Reach out | `ReachOutInput.attachments`: document ids from the step's own input (a fixed id, or an earlier step's output through a template), never written by a model. Checked when the plan is made (this client's, PDF or image; else `not_allowed` with the reason, nothing planned or sent), kept on the plan with names and sizes, shown on the plan's approval card, and sent with each attempt. // DECISION |
| A channel that can't carry them | Left out of the plan with the reason "can't carry the attachments" (core's `cannot_carry_attachments` block): a call carries none, WhatsApp one, email and MMS five. Never sent without the files, and never with a line naming files the person didn't get. None left: `not_allowed`, nothing sent. A WhatsApp message composed for a file is at most 1000 characters (Meta's caption). // DECISION |
| Email replies | `receiveReachOutReply` keeps the reply as a raw record (`ReachOutReply` on the client's upload connection, one per reach-out: headers, the reply's own words, each attachment's outcome) and its PDFs and images as `document(source='email')` with that raw record as their origin (`meta.rawRecordId`, `reachOutId`), in the reply's transaction. Then `document.received` is dispatched and the new documents read in the background, like forwarded mail. Checks as forwarded mail: types, inline images left out, magic bytes, 32 MB per document, at most 10 per reply. The reply's text is redacted for card numbers. A second delivery finds the reach-out ended and keeps nothing. // DECISION |
| Text and WhatsApp replies | The webhook (Twilio's and Meta's) starts capture's job for a message whose sender has an open reach-out (reason `reply`): its PDFs and images only, at the conversation's size limit (20 MB by default), fetched as capture fetches (Twilio's origin and redirect list, Meta's two-step download), filed once per media (`MessageMedia`), `source: 'message'`, the message's raw record as origin, read in the background. A text (MMS) has no conversation; its files are `mms-<sid>-<index>.<ext>`. // DECISION |
| Whatever the capture setting | A reply's PDFs and images are fetched even where the conversation keeps no attachments: the operator's reach-out asked this person for an answer. Nothing else of the setting changes, and other messages from them stay as the setting says. // DECISION |
| The reply waits for its files | A text or WhatsApp reply ends the reach-out as `replied` at once (escalation and the timeout stop), with `reply.pending` while a file has no outcome. The step waits on the same event; the reply's job, the step and the minute cron each settle it once every file has an outcome; after 15 minutes (`REPLY_MEDIA_WAIT_MS`) it is settled with what was filed and the rest counted as `not_fetched`. The cron doesn't re-wake a pending reply's run. // DECISION |
| The step's output | `ReachReply.documents`: the reply's documents' ids, for later steps (an empty list on replies stored before). `ReachReply.refused`: each attachment not kept, by type and reason (`type_not_allowed`, `too_large`, `type_mismatch`, `inline_image`, `too_many`, `not_fetched`): counted, never dropped silently. Text and files stay data (invariant 9): a document is read with `docs.*`. // DECISION |
| Compliance | No new vendor: Resend, Google, Microsoft, Twilio and Meta already carry the messages these files go with. |
| Not built | Files larger than Outlook's 3 MB (upload sessions). Meta's media upload. Media templates on WhatsApp. Attachments on a call. A reply's other types (audio, office files) as documents. Screens: the approval card shows `attachments` as it shows any before state; the owner's redesign gives them their own row. |

### 1.66 Conversations (P-4, M37; no migration; ARCHITECTURE §10.12)

Owner-approved 2026-11-07: an ingredient for agents the owner builds in the agent lab (the purchasing
agent first). No agent, template or workflow is built here. Hot-spot edits are blocks headed `// P-4: …`.

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Shape | Built-in skill `comms.converse` (`mode: 'tool'`, `reachesPeople`, handler `comms.converse` refusing to run outside a workflow step), routed by id in apps/api's agent port to `apps/api/src/converse/`, as `comms.reach-out` is. No new step kind and no executor change beyond one optional field: the executor's `waiting` and `approval` loop already lets a step wait and be approved any number of times. // DECISION |
| Config | `ConverseInput` in `packages/core/src/converse/` (ARCHITECTURE §10.12): `to` (`role`, `contactId` or `userId`, templates allowed), `channel` (`whatsapp`, `sms`, `email` for contacts; `portal` for client users), `goal` (text), `result` (JSON Schema, an object), `confirm = true`, `opening` (text), `whatsappTemplate?`, `about` (record ids), `data` (record data, as data), `limits {maxMessages 6 (1–12), waitPerReply '1 day' (≤ 7 days), deadline '3 days' (≤ 14 days), maxCostUsd '0.50' (≤ 5)}`. Fixed inputs: `goal`, `result`, `opening`. // DECISION: caps keep a conversation bounded whatever the operator types. |
| Validation | One function, `converseProblems`, in core's `workflow/validate.ts` (called from the entry point): the fixed inputs, the goal schema (an object with at least one property, at most 30), a channel that fits `to`, the limits. Template typing types `steps.<id>.output.result` from `result` (a skill may declare `resultFromInput: 'result'`; only built-ins do). // DECISION |
| Turns | `packages/agents/src/runtime/converse.ts`: one model call per turn through `packages/llm`, the agent's prompt as an `agent` skill gets it (instructions, memory notes, the client's language) plus the conversation preamble, the goal and the brief; the transcript and data in a tool-result block of a `read_conversation` call already made; `tool_choice` forces `turn` (`ask`, `confirm`, `done`, `give_up`). The model resolves as for an agent skill step (`resolveModel`), so the step's `model` wins. The result is checked against `result` in code. // DECISION |
| Confirmation | With `confirm`, `done` is accepted only after a `confirm` message has a reply and with `confirmed: true`; anything else fails the turn. Without it, `done` ends at once. // DECISION |
| Sending | One `reach_out` row per message (subject `converse:<run>:<node>`, node `<node>:m<n>`, `plan.converse {turn, kind, model, costUsd}`, the text as the plan's fixed message in the contact's language, the one channel, `expectReply`, timeout = the wait per reply), sent by reach out's `attemptFrom`: every check reach out makes, quiet hours held by the cron, key `<run>:<node>:m<n>:reach:0`. The first message's checks are `planReachOut` with the channel pinned (`urgency.normal: [channel]`). The daily cap counts a conversation's first message only. // DECISION |
| 24-hour window | Before a WhatsApp message, the contact's last inbound WhatsApp message is read: outside the window the opening goes as `whatsappTemplate` (`StoredPlan.whatsappTemplate`, sent as `whatsapp.send`'s `template`); a later message, or an opening without a template, ends the conversation (`not_allowed` before anything was sent, else a hand-over) with "outside WhatsApp's 24-hour window". // DECISION |
| Approval | The step's approval signs the conversation once (an inbox item `kind: approval`, payload `converse {who, channel, goal, limits, first message}`, routed like a reach-out plan); unsigned only under the three conditions and with no `approval` autonomy on the channel. Later messages go under it (`runPlannedAction`), except with the person's channel autonomy at `approval` or the agent in trial or sign-off: each message is then its own card (`payload.converse {turn, message, transcript}`, title "Message 3 of up to 6 to Dana on WhatsApp"). Approving sends exactly that text; declining is a rejected write (`declined`, the step's `onReject`). // DECISION |
| Replies | Reach out's matcher, unchanged except that it prefers an open conversation message on the reply's channel. One at a time per person: start refuses (`not_allowed`, "already waiting on them") while the person has an open reach-out or conversation, under an advisory lock on the contact; reach out's start does the same for someone in an open conversation. Between our messages, their texts (raw records on the channel's connection since the last one read) join the next turn; `routeInboundText` treats a conversation message awaiting approval as open, so no new run starts. // DECISION |
| Portal | With a client user: messages in the agent's thread (`agentThread`), `audience: client`, body `converse {runId, nodeId, turn, userId}`. The dispatcher matches `thread.message_posted` from that user in that thread while the step waits and wakes the run (`converse-<step run id>`); the reply is their messages after ours. // DECISION |
| Ending | `completed`, `opted_out`, `declined`, `not_allowed`, `handed_over`, `not_sent` (test runs). Limits (messages, a reply wait, the deadline, cost) and `give_up` hand over: an inbox item `kind: request`, subject `conversation_handover`, to whoever approves the conversation (the step's approver, the approver rules, the agent's, the client's setting, else the client admin; owner 2026-11-08: the client's team runs its agents day to day, the operator oversees; a test run's to the operator), with the goal, the last proposed result and the transcript (their words quoted). Answered → `handed_over` with the person's answer as `result` when it matches `result`; declined or timed out → the step's `onReject`. The executor's `PendingApproval` gains `waitingFor?: 'answer'` so the timeline says "waiting for an answer". // DECISION |
| Output | `ConverseOutput`: outcome, `result`, `confirmed`, `who {kind, id, name}`, channel, `messages`, `replies`, `transcript [{direction, text, at, sourceId}]`, `sources` (`about`, each reply's raw record or message id, each message's reach-out id), `reason`, `costUsd` (string). // DECISION |
| Durability | The opening turn in the step's own `do`; turn *n* in `…:wait:<k>:resume` or `…:approval:<k>:resume`. The answer is stored on the message row (insert, unique on run and node) before sending; a retried `do` finds it and sends under the same key. Oversight: a failed turn fails the step; `retry` runs the step again and the service picks the conversation up where it stopped (the conversation is keyed by `keyNode ?? nodeId`, so a `retry_with` starts afresh once the old one has no open message). // DECISION |
| Storage | No migration: `reach_out`, the contact's thread lines, `message` rows in the agent's thread, `model_call` rows, the step's cost. // DECISION: every table that holds a conversation's parts already exists, with RLS and its rules. |
| Visibility | `GET /api/ops/runs/:id`: a conversation step gains `conversation {channel, who, outcome, messages [{turn, direction, text, at, status, model?, costUsd?}]}`. `GET /api/runs/:id` (portal): `conversation {messages [{direction, text, at}]}` only; the contract test's forbidden-field check covers it. |
| Builder | Palette "Talk with a person" (`comms.converse`); inspector: who, channel, what to find out (field list → `result`), the goal's words, the first message, the WhatsApp template, limits, and the model picker skill steps already have. |
| Compliance | No new vendor: the model and the channels' providers are already named in `packages/core/src/compliance`. |
| Not in this stream | Voice (calls stay with reach out and the voice agent); a person changing the message before approving it (W1-D's edit applies to external actions, not these cards; built in P-6, §1.68); translating replies (§1.18); the client's lead (an operator) as `to` (open, owner). |

Decisions made while building M37 (`claude/p4-conversation`, 2026-11-07; `// P-4` blocks):

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Input shape | Flat, because a skill's fixed inputs are top-level fields: `goal` (text), `result` (the JSON Schema), `confirm`, `opening` (text), `whatsappTemplate`, beside `to`, `channel`, `about`, `data`, `limits`. Fixed: `goal`, `result`, `opening`. // DECISION |
| Signed messages | Every message after the first is signed while the agent isn't live (bench, trial, sign-off), not only in trial and sign-off: a run of a bench agent is a person trying it, and model-written words to a real person get a look. // DECISION |
| A step's model | Allowed on `comms.converse` although it runs as code: each turn is a model call in the agent's voice. The builder's model row shows for it. // DECISION |
| Quiet hours | At the start, a conversation whose one channel is only blocked by quiet hours is held (the cron sends its first message when they end, and the reply wait starts then), not refused. // DECISION |
| "YES" and "HELP" | Inside a conversation they are the person's answer (to "Is that right?") as well as Twilio's opt-in and help words; STOP still stops everything. Outside one, as before. // DECISION |
| Daily cap | A conversation counts once (its first message) toward the person's cap, in both the conversation's start and reach out's. // DECISION |
| Oversight | A failed turn is reviewed although the step's messages reached the client's tool: `retry` picks the conversation up where it stopped (sent messages are keyed and never resent); `retry_with` is not offered for it, since it would start over with the person. The finish tool offers `retry`, `ask`, `stop`. // DECISION |
| The model and a crash | The turn's answer is stored on the message row it writes. A crash after the model call and before that insert calls the model again for that turn (a cost, never a second send). // DECISION |
| Closing | `done.closing` is sent only when messages go under the conversation's signature; with each message signed, there is no closing message. // DECISION |
| Hand-over item | `kind: request`, `subjectType: conversation_handover`, `payload.mode: ask`, the transcript with the person's lines marked `theirWords`; to whoever approves the conversation, so the client's team by default. Answered with a result that fits `result` → that result; answered otherwise → `handed_over` with no result; declined → the step's `onReject`. // DECISION |
| Run pages | Each message's model and cost are kept on its row (`plan.converse`); a turn that wrote no message (`done`, `give_up`, a failure) adds its cost to the message it answered. The portal shows only messages that went and replies, never one awaiting a signature. The portal's lines read "Sent" and "Reply" (six languages; the five other than English need a fluent review). // DECISION |
| Not built | The portal channel (a client user in the agent's thread): `start` answers `not_allowed`, "talking in the portal isn't built yet", and the builder offers only directory people and roles. Reading messages the person sends between ours (their texts while no message of ours is open) at the next turn. A reply arriving in the seconds between one turn and the next message isn't matched (it is kept in the contact's thread as any text). |

### 1.67 Purchasing area v2 (P-5, M38; migration 0033)

The Materials pack's next version (0.2.0, same pack id `materials`) gives the Purchasing area what a
purchasing agent needs: requests to buy, the vendors that sold an item, requests for quotes and
their comparison, a purchase order PDF, and prices from QuickBooks bills. The owner builds the agent
in the Agent lab. QuickBooks writes (P-2), attachments on sent messages (P-3) and two-way
conversations (P-4) are other streams.

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Migration | One migration, 0033 (the lead reserved 0035; 0033 was still free when it merged, §8.1). Four tables, each with `tenant_id`, forced RLS, `cs_runtime` grants without DELETE, composite tenant foreign keys and the `pack:materials` comment: `purchase_request`, `purchase_request_line`, `quote_request`, `quote_line`. Additive only. |
| Request | Who asked: a contact or a user, at most one, neither when an agent opened it. Channel `email`, `sms`, `whatsapp`, `portal` or `agent`; the raw record it came from; needed-by; `delivery` or `pickup` with where; the order's reference (connector and external id) once placed; run and agent version. A test run's request is written and marked (`ext.testRun`), and lists leave it out. // DECISION |
| Status | `open`, `quoting`, `quoted`, `ordered`, `closed` only move forward (skipping is allowed); `cancelled` from any status before `ordered`. The first RFQ sent makes a request `quoting`; it is `quoted` once a quote is in and no RFQ sent is still waiting. An order's reference makes it `ordered`, once: a different reference is refused. Lines are added only while `open` or `quoting`. // DECISION |
| Matching request lines | Deterministic only, through the catalog's own keys and rules: with no vendor, every vendor's description alias for the text, when they all name one item; then exactly one candidate equal on every key attribute. Anything else keeps up to five candidates and no item, never a guess. A request never creates a catalog item. A person's choice (`choices`) may name any live item, recorded as `chosen`. // DECISION |
| Quote | One RFQ per vendor and request (`unique`); asking again finds it. `materials.quote.create` drafts and sends nothing; `mark-sent` records how it went out. A quoted line without a unit or quantity is for the unit and quantity asked. The standard-unit price is set only through a conversion rule, else null with the reason. An answer is recorded once: a retried step gets it back flagged `alreadyRecorded`; a revised answer is a new RFQ. A quote is not a price observation: price history stays what was paid. // DECISION |
| Compare | In code, never a model. Per line, offers compare in the item's standard unit; an offer whose unit has no rule compares only with offers in the very same unit, and never across currencies (the currency most offers are in wins; a tie compares nothing). A vendor's total is for the lines as asked. A vendor that would deliver after needed-by is suggested only when no on-time vendor quoted every line. A split is suggested when it saves at least `split_min_saving_pct` (5) on the best single vendor, or when no vendor quoted every line. Reasons are plain sentences; sources are the request, its lines, the quotes and every offer. // DECISION |
| Purchase order | `materials.po.document` renders the PDF with pdf-lib (as the Weekly Table's) from a request and a received quote: the client's name, the vendor, the lines the vendor can supply with amounts and total in decimal, needed-by, delivery or pickup, a reference (given, else `PO-` and the request id's first eight characters). Fixed dates make the same order the same bytes, so it is one document. Stored with a new document source, `generated`, and no `document.received` event (it is ours, not something to read). Pack tools get the content store through `packServices.content`. // DECISION |
| Backfill | `materials.backfill-from-bills` reads synced QuickBooks bills by date (at most 500 a call, `next` continues), matches item lines deterministically (a new item when the rules say so), learns the vendor's alias, and records a price per bill line keyed by its raw record and QBO line id (`price_observation.raw_record_id`, `raw_line_id`, unique). A line already priced from an earlier version of the bill is not priced again. Bill lines carry no unit: the price is per the item's standard unit, marked `uomAssumed`. Account lines and unsure lines are counted and left; no events (history, not news). What it can't use: `packs/materials/NOTES.md`. // DECISION |
| Reading skills | `materials.read-request` and `materials.read-quote` are `task` skills on the classification model: the text is the task input (data, invariant 9); they call the deterministic tools (`materials.catalog.match-text`, `materials.quote.get`, `docs.get`) and never pick a candidate or convert a unit. Their outputs feed `materials.request.create` and `materials.quote.record` directly (null means "not stated"). // DECISION |
| Tools and skills | Tools (`none` or `internal`, no approval, logged with the step): `materials.request.{create,get,list,update,add-lines,match-lines}`, `materials.catalog.match-text`, `materials.vendors-for-items`, `materials.frequent-items`, `materials.quote.{create,mark-sent,get,record,compare}`, `materials.po.document`, `materials.backfill-from-bills`. Tool skills (category Purchasing): open-request, update-request, match-request, find-vendors, top-items, request-quotes, mark-quote-sent, record-quote, compare-quotes, purchase-order, import-bill-prices. Quantities and prices take decimal strings or JSON numbers read as their shortest decimal text, at most 4 places. // DECISION |
| Reports | A `requests` report (JSON and CSV) under the generic pack report routes; no screen: the UI session owns screens. |
| Files | `packs/materials/src/purchasing/*`, `packs/materials/NOTES.md`; one block each in `packs/materials/src/{schema,manifest,tools,api,index}.ts`, `apps/api/src/packs.ts`, `apps/api/src/runs/execute.ts`; `apps/api/src/purchasing-area.test.ts`. |

### 1.68 Client-run, operator steps in (P-6, M39; no migration; ARCHITECTURE §4, §10.12, §11)

Owner, 2026-11-08: the client's team runs its agents day to day; the operator oversees and can step into
anything, but is never the default step. Hot-spot edits are blocks headed `// P-6: …`.

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Stepping in | `POST /api/ops/clients/:id/inbox/:itemId/step-in {action: approve, reject, answer or reassign, reason, note?, message?, answer?, to?}`, operators only, for an open item of a client they serve (any other client's, or an item under the wrong client: 404). The reason is required. The client is in the path, so `mount`'s audit row names it. // DECISION |
| The client's path | Approve, reject and answer call `resolveInboxItem` with the operator as approver and `steppingIn: true`, a flag only this route sets on the server. `mayResolve` lets it through as it does the Sandbox flag; nothing else changes. A change is approved once (`resolveExternalActionTx`), held by the undo window, and written once by the run (`executeAction`, its idempotency key, its before state). // DECISION |
| What is kept | `payload.steppedIn {action, reason, at}` (the portal reads it), `resolution.steppedIn` with who (the back office only). The portal's item and run page say "We stepped in: {reason}" (portal voice: "we" for Mezonas, copper as a person's action) in six languages and give `resolvedBy.name` as null: we never name our people to the client. // DECISION |
| Refused | A request for a code (`access_release`, 409 `enter_code`): only the client's own code releases it (§1.26). The go-live sign-off (409 `client_sign_off`): it is the client's acceptance of its agent. A change request and a Sloan account change keep their own routes (as `resolveInboxItem` already refuses); a Sloan account change and a directory suggestion are the client admin's and aren't passed on (409 `not_reassignable`). An approval we make and then undo in its window no longer reads as ours. // DECISION |
| Reassign | To a client role (`client_admin`, `client_user`) or a person of that client who signs (else 422 `not_a_member` or `not_a_signer`). The item keeps `payload.reassigned [{from, to, reason, at, byUs}]`. // DECISION |
| Telling them | The existing path to the person an item waited on is the lead thread's notification (`lead_message`), so stepping in posts one line there from us, in the client's language (core `stepInLine`): "We stepped in on "{title}" and approved it for you. Why: {reason}". Not for test runs or our own items. // DECISION |
| HIPAA | Allowed, and audited like every session route: stepping in sends no client data to a new vendor, so `packages/core/src/compliance` has nothing to refuse. // DECISION |
| Conversations | `POST /api/ops/clients/:id/runs/:runId/conversations/:stepRunId {action: take_over or end, reason}` (operator, reason required) and `POST /api/runs/:id/conversations/:stepRunId {action, reason?}` (the conversation's approver, as its approval would be routed now; viewers and other client users 403; test runs 404 in the portal). In the request's transaction, no model call. // DECISION |
| Take over | The message waiting is ended unsent (`declined`, reason "taken over …"); a hand-over item (`conversation_handover`) goes to that person (the operator's own item when it is us); the step waits on it and their answer is the result, as in §1.66. A message card waiting for approval is withdrawn: resolved with `withdrawn: true`, which the executor reads as no decision (`InboxResolution.withdrawn`), so the later answer isn't a rejection. A hand-over already open is reassigned to the person. // DECISION |
| End | The waiting message is ended (`declined`, "ended by …"); a message card or hand-over waiting is declined. The step ends `declined`, and the executor applies its `onReject` (`StepOutcome.done.declined`). // DECISION |
| Between messages | In the seconds between a reply and the agent's next message there is nothing to stop yet: 409 `busy`, try again, rather than a flag the run must find later. // DECISION |
| Transcript | Take-overs and ends are `plan.converse.events [{kind, at, by, byUs, reason}]` on the conversation's last row. The back office shows the person's name; the portal shows "Mezonas" for us and the client's people by name, and offers the buttons only to the conversation's approver (`mayControl`). // DECISION |
| Editing a message | The approver of a conversation's message or a reach-out's plan edits it with the Desk's shape, `PUT /api/inbox/:id/edit {input: {message}}`; the operator stepping in sends `message` with `approve`. The edit becomes the row's fixed message (both languages' keys), so what is approved is exactly what is sent; the agent's text is kept in `plan.edited {original, text, by, byUs, at}` and the card's `payload.edited`. A second edit keeps the first original. // DECISION |
| Checks on an edit | At the edit: not empty, at most the channel's length (a conversation's, or reach out's per channel), card numbers redacted. At the send: the same path as any message, so consent, an opt-out, quiet hours (held), the matrix and the idempotency key all hold. A call's words can't be edited (409 `not_editable`). An edited conversation opening loses its WhatsApp template: outside the 24-hour window the send is refused rather than the template going in place of the approved words. // DECISION |
| Defaults audit | Oversight's `ask` with no step approver went to the lead: now the client's team (the agent's approver, the client's setting, else the client admin). The conversation hand-over already went to the conversation's approver (§1.66). Kept as they are, because they are opt-in or oversight: a person step assigned to the lead or to `operator`, a client whose approvals are set to us, a person step's `notify_operator` timeout, test runs' items, failed writes, budget hits, oversight stops and proposals, supervisor findings, code alerts and browser stops (both sides), change requests, the Materials pack's catalog merge proposals (a merge is a back-office action), and email or digest notices (operator-only thread notes until a sender is wired: a notice, not work). // DECISION |
| Not in this stream | Stepping in from the MCP connector; a "step in" button on the run page's change panels (the Escalations screen's "Waiting on clients" view has it); editing a call's words. |

### 1.69 Message translation (P-7, M40; migration 0034; ARCHITECTURE §10.13, §5.7)

Owner, 2026-11-08: "start message translation". §1.18's next step comes into v1. A client can keep its
work in one working language: messages in another language get a translation stored beside the
original, and a person's words to a contact can go in the contact's language. Hot-spot edits are
blocks headed `// P-7: …`.

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Off by default | `tenant.settings.translation` absent, or `working: null`, is off: nothing is detected, stored, called or shown differently, and every existing test and fixture is unchanged. // DECISION: a client turns it on; nobody's messages start costing model calls unasked. |
| Client settings | `tenant.settings.translation = {working, incoming, outgoing, threads, model?, dailyLimit, enabledAt}` (core `TranslationSettings`, Zod). `working`: one of the six. `incoming`, `outgoing`, `threads` default `true` once `working` is set. `model` defaults to the classification model; it must be a priced id (`knownModelIds()`). `dailyLimit` defaults to 200 (1–5,000). `enabledAt` is set when `working` goes from unset to set. No migration: the settings are JSON like the client's language. |
| Who sets them | The operator: `GET/PUT /api/ops/clients/:id/translation` (all fields, a reason). The client admin: `GET/PUT /api/translation` (`working` and the three switches; the model and the limit are ours, because they are cost). Every save writes a `tenant_change` row (`setting: translation`, from, to, the actor), as approver rules do. Client users read them. // DECISION |
| Per contact | `contact.translation = {incoming?, outgoing?}` (migration 0034, default `{}`): `true` or `false` overrides the client's switch for that person; absent follows the client. `PUT /api/contacts/:id/translation` (client admin) and `PUT /api/ops/clients/:id/contacts/:contactId/translation` (operator), logged in `contact_change` as autonomy is. The person's language stays §1.27's `languageFor` (theirs, their organization's, the client's). // DECISION |
| What comes in | Texts and WhatsApp (Twilio and Meta), email replies to a reach-out, email to an agent's address, and a client user's text message in a thread. Conversation replies are texts, so they are covered. Into the working language; a client user's thread message into English, for the back office (which stays English, §1.18). // DECISION |
| Webhooks | Not automatic: a webhook payload is arbitrary JSON, and which field is a person's words is the step's to say. An agent translates a field with the tool (`sourceId` the delivery's raw record, `field` its path). // DECISION |
| Detecting | `detectLanguage(text)` in core, pure code, no regular expressions on the text: letters are counted by code point; mostly non-Latin letters is `unknown` (another script); otherwise common words of each of the six (a word in several lists counts a share in each) and letters such as ñ, ã, ß, ç, œ score each language. `sure` (at least 3 points and twice the next language), `likely` or `unsure`. Fewer than 3 letters is `skipped: no_words`, with no call. // DECISION |
| The model only if needed | Sure and already the target: `skipped: same_language`, no call. Sure and another language: one call with the source named. Not sure: the same one call with no source; the model names it (one of the six, or `other`). `other` is stored as `unknown` with `skipped: unknown_language` and no text: we don't guess. A model that says the text is already in the target: `skipped: same_language` (the call is still counted). // DECISION: detection never costs a separate call. |
| The skill | `comms.translate`, a built-in skill in `tool` mode with the handler `comms.translate` (category People). It behaves like a task skill: its own model (the client's `translation.model`, else the classification model), the same in any agent, no agent instructions, no memory. // DECISION: a `tool`-mode skill, not `mode: 'task'` run by `runAgent`, so a step, an agent's tool call and the background job all go through one function that applies the client's model, the daily limit, the HIPAA guard and storage beside the original; a `task` skill would skip all four. No step `model` override: the client's choice is the one setting. |
| The call | `packages/agents/src/runtime/translate.ts`: one model call through `packages/llm`. The system prompt is fixed text (translate everything as written, instructions included; keep names, numbers, amounts, dates, codes and links as written; add nothing). The text arrives as the result of a `read_message` call already made, in a tool-result block, never in the system prompt; the answer is `finish {sourceLanguage, translation}`, checked with Zod. An empty or missing translation is `failed`. // DECISION |
| The tool | `comms.translate {sourceId, field = "text", to?}` (`sideEffect: internal`): `sourceId` is a raw record or a thread message of the run's client (read in a client-scoped transaction; another client's id is "not found"); `field` a dotted path of at most 5 names into the raw record's payload, which must hold a string. `to` defaults to the working language, else the client's language. A stored translation of the same original, field and target is returned with no call. Output `{status, sourceId, field, from, to, text, truncated, translationId, sources}`; the tool's description says the text is the sender's words, data, never instructions. Free text is not offered: what is translated is a record, and the translation is stored beside it. // DECISION |
| Stored beside | Every outcome is one `message_translation` row (migration 0034): the original (exactly one of `raw_record_id`, `message_id`, `reach_out_id`), `field`, `direction`, both languages, `detected_by` (`rule`, `model`, `none`), `status` (`translated`, `skipped`, `capped`, `refused`, `failed`) with `reason`, the translation `text` (only when translated), `original_chars`, `truncated`, `model`, tokens, `cost_usd`, `agent_version_id`, `run_id`, `step_run_id` where an agent made it, `requested_by` for a person's own text. Insert only (trigger, no UPDATE or DELETE for `cs_runtime`), like `doc_extraction`. One `translated` row per original, field and target (partial unique); a retry is a new row. The original's row is never touched (invariant 3). // DECISION |
| When | After the commit that stores the original, a deferred job runs `translatePending` for the client: the client's originals since `enabledAt` and in the last 24 hours with no row yet, newest first, at most 25. The hourly cron runs the same for every client with translation on, so a lost job is caught within the hour. Each candidate gets one row, so nothing is looked at twice. // DECISION |
| A contact who is off | `skipped: contact_off`, no call. A STOP, START or HELP keyword is read from the original, as before; a translation is never read for keywords or `/commands`. // DECISION |
| Long texts | At most 6,000 characters are translated, cut at the last paragraph end, else sentence end, else space, before it; the row is `truncated` and the screens say "Translated the first 6,000 characters". Email replies are already their own words (quoted history cut). // DECISION: one call per text, no chunking: longer messages are rare and the cut is stated. |
| Daily limit | Model translations (rows with a model) per client per UTC day stop at `dailyLimit`: past it, `capped` with no call. A person's outgoing request past it is refused (429 `translation_limit`). The tool answers `capped`. Counted before the call, so jobs running at once may pass it by a few. // DECISION |
| HIPAA | Every call goes through `guardModels` (a run's transport already is, through `forClient`; the job and the routes wrap theirs). A refusal is `refused` (the screens say the model isn't covered for this client's data) and makes no call. // DECISION |
| Usage | Each row keeps its model, tokens and cost. Rows with no agent version (the job, a person's request) roll up into `usage_platform_daily` (new `translations` column, plus tokens and cost) and count into credits with it. A tool call inside a run is that step's model call (`recordModelCall`) and its cost the step's, so it is counted once, in `usage_daily`. // DECISION |
| Untrusted text | The translation is the sender's words in another language: stored and shown as theirs (quoted), handed to agents only in tool results, never in instructions (invariant 9). A test sends a foreign-language message that tries to give orders; it is translated as text, and nothing it says is done. // DECISION |
| Outgoing: the Desk | `PUT /api/inbox/:id/edit {input: {message, translate: true}}` (P-6's edit): the card numbers are cut first, the person's words are translated into the contact's language, and the translation becomes the message (both languages' keys, as P-6), so what is approved is exactly what is sent. The plan and the card keep `translated {original, language, translationId}`; the approver sees both. Already in the contact's language: saved as written (`skipped: same_language`). Too long once translated: 422 `too_long`. The client's or the contact's `outgoing` off: 409 `translation_off`. Without `translate`, P-6 exactly. // DECISION |
| Outgoing: the lead | `POST /api/ops/threads/:id/messages {…, translate: true}`: the lead's words are posted as written, and their translation into the working language is stored beside them (`direction: out`); the client reads the translation with the lead's words one click away. Nothing in a thread is signed. // DECISION |
| Not translated again | Agent-written messages (reach out, conversations) are composed in the contact's language already, and fixed templates carry a text per language: neither goes through translation. Only words a person writes do. // DECISION |
| Not offered | Stepping in with an edit (§1.68): we write in the contact's language or ask the client's approver to edit. There is no manual send to a contact today; one that lands uses the Desk's shape. // DECISION |
| Who sees what | Each place that shows an original gains `translation {status, from, to, text, truncated}` (the latest row for it): the contact's messages (both surfaces), thread messages (both), conversation transcripts (both run pages) and the Desk's card (`payload.translated`). The back office also gets `model` and `costUsd`. The portal gets neither, nor a prompt; the contract test's forbidden-field check covers the new fields. // DECISION |
| Screens | One shared piece: the translation first, marked "Translated from {language}", with "Show original" one click away (design system classes, six-language strings). The UI session owns the screens; this stream touches only what shows a message's text. |
| Isolation | Rows have `tenant_id` and forced RLS; every query also filters by it; settings, limits and counts are per client; the tool reads only its run's client. Client B never sees client A's translations (test). // DECISION |
| Compliance | No new vendor: the model's provider is already named in `packages/core/src/compliance`. |
| Not in this stream | Documents and invoices (never translated, §1.18), call audio and transcripts, live call translation, right-to-left languages, languages outside the six, a "try again" button for a capped or failed translation, translating webhook payloads without a step. |

Decisions made while building M40 (`claude/p7-translation`, 2026-11-08; `// P-7` blocks):

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Where the code is | Core: `translate/detect.ts` (detection), `translate/settings.ts` (settings, the contact's switch, the cut, payload paths, the views), `api/translation.ts` (six routes). Agents: `runtime/translate.ts` (the call and its prompt), `tools/translate.ts` (the tool, the skill, the port's shape), `log/translations.ts` (`translateAndStore`, the one path for every caller, and the views' reads), `log/translation-port.ts`. The Worker: `translate/service.ts` (the job, the sweep, settings, the Desk's helper), `translate/views.ts`, `routes/translation.ts`. // DECISION |
| Every candidate gets a row | The job reads the client's originals whatever the client's switch, because a contact's own switch can turn it on for them; one that isn't wanted is `skipped` with `client_off` or `contact_off` and no call, so the next pass never reads it again and a chatty contact can't crowd out others. // DECISION |
| The sweep | Hourly at minute 23 (`TRANSLATION_SWEEP_MINUTE`), every client whose `translation.working` is set. After a pass that called the model, the client's `usage_platform_daily` for the day is rolled up again. // DECISION |
| Thread messages | A client member's text message (sender a `client_admin` or `client_user` of that client, kind `text`) is translated into English. The portal never shows that translation (it is for us); it shows a lead message's (`direction: out`). A `/command` message is never translated. // DECISION |
| The lead's reply | `translate: true` on a note for operators is refused (422 `not_for_client`); with the client's working language or outgoing switch off, 409 `translation_off`. The translation is made after the response, like the job. // DECISION |
| The Desk | The translation is made in the edit's request, before the message row is locked, so the approver gets the card with both texts in the answer. Only an open item is translated; the person's words must be a valid message first. A translation the limit, HIPAA or a failure stops is an error and nothing is saved: 429 `translation_limit`, 409 `not_covered`, 502 `translation_failed`, 422 `not_translatable`. The plan's and the card's `edited.translated` is `{original, from, to, translationId}`. // DECISION |
| No model configured | Where no model key is set (and in tests by default), a call that would be made is stored as `failed: no_model`; nothing else changes. // DECISION |
| Tokens and cost | The row keeps the call's tokens (cache included) and the cost to four places, like a document's reading. // DECISION |
| Tool output | `sources` is the original's id and the translation row's id. The step's output keeps `from`, `to`, `text` and `status`, so later steps can read the translation as data. // DECISION |
| Screens | One shared piece, `apps/web/src/ui/translated.tsx` (`TranslatedText`), used in the contact's message list (both surfaces). The thread, conversation and Desk screens get the fields from the API and are the UI session's. Six languages' words, `translation.*`; the five other than English need the fluent review. // DECISION |
| Not built | A test of the conversation transcripts' `translation` (the views are typed and share `translationsFor`); a "try again" for a capped or failed translation; translation on the step-in edit. |

Open for the owner (the defaults chosen):
- Off for every client until the operator or the client admin sets a working language.
- With a working language set, incoming, outgoing and thread translation are on; each can be turned off for the client, and incoming and outgoing per contact.
- The model is the classification model (the small, cheap one); the operator can pick another per client.
- 200 model translations per client per day.
- At most 6,000 characters per message; the rest is cut and marked.
- Texts under 3 letters are not translated; a language outside the six is stored as unknown.
- The catch-up looks back 24 hours.
- Translations count as the client's usage at model cost, like background document reading.
- The lead's translated reply shows the client the translation first.
- No translation when we step in with an edit.
- The new strings in five languages need a fluent speaker's review, like §1.18's.

### 1.70 US texting registration (P-8, M41; no migration; ARCHITECTURE §1 "Texts and calls")

Owner, 2026-11-08: US texts need A2P 10DLC registration, and Mezonas does it by Twilio's API once it
has the client's details. Fees are passed to the client as credits, never absorbed. The client
admin enters the business details in the portal; the operator reviews them, sees the fees and
submits from the back office. Use cases are never marketing: the company's own texts to its team,
its vendors and its customers (customer care). Twilio's facts, each with its source page, are in
`packages/connectors/src/twilio/NOTES.md` ("A2P 10DLC by API (P-8)", checked 2026-11-08 against
twilio.com's live docs). Hot-spot edits are blocks headed `// P-8: …`.

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| The flow (Twilio's ISV guide, architecture #1: a subaccount and a Messaging Service per client) | In the client's subaccount, with its credentials: (1) the **secondary customer profile** (Trust Hub `CustomerProfiles`, policy `RNdfbf3fae0e1107f8aded0e7cead80bf5`): the business's end user (`customer_profile_business_information`), the authorized representative (`authorized_representative_1`), the address (an `Address` and a `customer_profile_address` supporting document), an assignment to Mezonas' primary profile, an evaluation, then `pending-review`; (2) the **A2P trust product** (`TrustProducts`, policy `RNb0d4771c2c98518d916a3d4cd70a8f8b`) with its `us_a2p_messaging_profile_information` end user and the profile assigned, evaluated, then `pending-review`; (3) the **BrandRegistration**; (4) a new **Messaging Service** for the client; (5) once the brand is `APPROVED`, the **Usa2p campaign** on that service; (6) once the campaign is `VERIFIED`, the client's US numbers **added to the service**. Steps 1–4 run in the operator's submit; 5 and 6 on the cron. // DECISION |
| Use case | `MIXED` for a Standard brand, `LOW_VOLUME` for a Low-Volume Standard brand. Twilio's "Gather business information" page lists `MIXED` as "a campaign that covers multiple use cases such as Customer Care and Delivery Notifications" and `LOW_VOLUME` as the same for low-volume brands at a lower monthly fee; our traffic is internal (codes and work updates to the team), vendor (orders, deliveries, invoices) and customer care, which no single use case covers. The use case is set in code from the brand type, never from input, and checked against the brand's `Compliance/Usa2p/Usecases` list before the campaign is made. One campaign per client (Twilio allows five per brand). // DECISION |
| Marketing refused | The form's uses are `team`, `vendors` and `customer_care` only; a body naming `marketing` (as a use or the use case) is refused with 422 `marketing_refused`, and so is an operator's edit of the campaign's description or samples that reads as promotion (whole words: promo, promotion, promotional, discount, coupon, marketing, advertising, advertisement). Description and samples say "no marketing or promotional messages". // DECISION |
| Brand type | The client's answer: "fewer than 6,000 texts a day" (the default) is **Low-Volume Standard** (`BrandType=STANDARD`, `SkipAutomaticSecVet=true`, use case `LOW_VOLUME`); more is **Standard** (`MIXED`). Sole proprietors without an EIN, public companies (stock ticker and Authentication+ email) and the mock flag are not offered. Company type: `private`, `non-profit` or `government`. // DECISION |
| What the client admin enters | Portal `/settings/texting`, a Settings section (client admin only; others read nothing): legal name exactly as on the EIN letter, business type, industry, company type, website, regions (default USA and Canada), address (US), the authorized representative (first and last name, title, position, phone in E.164, email), volume, uses, how people agree to texts (40–2,048 characters, in the client's own words; we never invent the opt-in), the privacy policy and terms links, and whether texts carry links or phone numbers. Validated with Zod in core (`A2pBusinessDetails`), the same schema the API and the screen use. The form is open until the operator submits, and again when Twilio asks for changes. // DECISION |
| The EIN | Write-only and encrypted with the client's own key: it is a field of the client's `twilio` connection credentials (the vault, §1.26), next to the subaccount's SID and token, which the connector's own parse leaves out. Setting it decrypts the stored credentials in the request and writes them back with the EIN, the one merge §1.26's form rule doesn't cover, because those other fields are ours, not the form's. Read models say only `einSet` and a masked hint (`••-•••••89`); no route returns it, and `ein`, `taxId` and `businessRegistrationNumber` join the contract's forbidden secret fields. It reaches Twilio only in the end user's attributes, is redacted from Twilio's error text before anything is stored or shown, and never reaches a model, a log, a step output or a screen. Disconnecting drops it with the credentials. // DECISION |
| Where state lives | No migration. The registration's state is `connection.config.a2p.registration` on the client's `twilio` connection (details without the EIN, the hint, stage, every SID, statuses, Twilio's reasons, submissions with who and why, fee keys recorded); the existing `a2p` fields (brand, Messaging Service, statuses, `status`) keep their meaning, so today's checks and screens work unchanged. Every change is a `connection_change` row naming fields, never values. // DECISION |
| Stages | `draft` → `submitted` (profile, trust product and brand with Twilio) → `campaign` (brand approved, campaign with TCR) → `ready` (campaign verified, numbers in the service: "Texting is ready"); `needs_changes` when Twilio rejects anything (with its reasons); `suspended` (brand or campaign suspended: Twilio support). `a2p.status` is `approved` only at `ready`, so US texts stay refused until then, as today. // DECISION |
| Who submits | Only the operator (`POST /api/ops/clients/:id/messaging/a2p/submit`, OPS roles, a client they serve), with a required reason and `confirmFees: true`, after reading the fees. A client admin's call is 403. The operator may edit the campaign's description, samples and opt-in text before submitting; the client's details stay the client's. Each submit is recorded (`submissions [{at, by, reason}]`) and audited by `mount`. // DECISION |
| Fees (Twilio's A2P 10DLC page, read 2026-11-08) | Shown before each submit, with what this submit will charge: Standard brand $44 one-time, Low-Volume Standard brand $4 one-time, campaign vetting $15 one-time per campaign, then the campaign's monthly fee (Twilio says $1.50–$10 by use case; we use $10 for `MIXED` and $1.50 for `LOW_VOLUME`, from TCR's published rates as resellers quote them: Twilio's help article could not be read, NOTES.md). The amounts are a table in core (`A2P_FEES`), with its source and date. // DECISION |
| Fees as the client's usage | Each fee is a raw record `ProviderFee` on the twilio connection (our own fact, like `MessageAttempt`), keyed once: `a2p:brand:<BN>`, `a2p:vetting:<MG>:<attempt>`, `a2p:monthly:<MG>:<YYYY-MM>`, `on conflict do nothing`. The platform rollup (`rollupPlatformDaily`) adds the day's `ProviderFee` amounts to `usage_platform_daily.cost_usd`, the client's own usage, which billing already turns into credits (`cost_usd` at 1.0000 per 1: one credit per cent). So no fee is billed twice, none is missed, and no new rule or column is needed; the entity type is platform-wide (`PROVIDER_FEE_ENTITY` in core), so any connector's pass-through fee works the same way. The monthly fee is recorded at the first poll of each UTC month while the campaign is `VERIFIED`. // DECISION |
| Status polling | Hourly on the cron (minute :23, `A2P_CRON_MINUTE`), for each client whose registration is `submitted` or `campaign`, one `withTenant` per client; the back office's "Check status" runs the same pass at once. Twilio asks for at most one brand or campaign request a second; a pass makes at most one of each per client. // DECISION |
| Telling people | Plain words, as `review` inbox items (subject `a2p_registration`, the connection) for the operator and the client admin, once per stage: "Texting is ready" (ready), "Twilio needs changes to your texting registration" with Twilio's reasons and what to fix (needs changes), "Texting is on hold" (suspended). An older open item for the registration is closed when a new one is raised. // DECISION |
| Rejections | Twilio's reasons are kept as `{source, code, fields, description}`: a profile or trust product evaluation's failed fields, a `twilio-rejected` bundle's errors, a brand's `errors[]` and a campaign's `errors[]` (with Twilio's error code and link). Each is shown with what to fix, in plain words: the fields to change and where (the client's details, or the campaign's text). // DECISION |
| Re-submits | After an edit, the operator submits again. Profile, trust product or brand rejected: the end users and address are updated in place and both bundles go back to `pending-review`, then the brand is updated (`POST BrandRegistrations/{BN}`, free up to three times; a fourth is refused with Twilio's 21724 and the operator is told to ask Twilio support). Campaign rejected: Twilio's edit-in-place is a private beta for API users, so the campaign is deleted and made again, which charges a new $15 vetting fee, shown before the submit and recorded under the next attempt. // DECISION |
| Sending once approved | Texts from a number in the client's Messaging Service go with `MessagingServiceSid` **and** `From` (Twilio: both together keep the sender we chose and still use the service). Release-code texts too. A number bought later is added to the service when the campaign is verified. WhatsApp is unchanged. A client registered by hand in Twilio's console (the old recorded SIDs) keeps sending `From` alone. // DECISION |
| Deployment settings | `TWILIO_PRIMARY_PROFILE_SID` (Mezonas' approved ISV primary profile, BU…) and `TWILIO_A2P_EMAIL` (Mezonas' own address for Trust Hub's status emails; Twilio says not the customer's). Not secrets. Without them the submit answers 503 `not_configured`. // DECISION |
| Portal answers | The portal's view has the stage, the details (the client's own), `einSet`, the hint, whether it can be edited and Twilio's reasons in plain words: no SID, no fee, no cost. The contract's portal check covers it. // DECISION |
| Not in this stream | Toll-free verification, sole proprietors, public companies, Twilio's mock brand from code (a manual checklist is in NOTES.md), Event Streams (we poll), the $10 brand appeal, Twilio's campaign edit-in-place beta, numbers outside the US. |

#### Decisions made while building P-8 (2026-11-08)

Built on `claude/p8-a2p` (stacked on this design). No migration.

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Where the code is | Twilio's calls in `packages/connectors/src/twilio/a2p.ts` (each answer parsed with Zod); the flow, fees, notices and the cron pass in `apps/api/src/messaging/a2p.ts`; the portal routes in `apps/api/src/routes/a2p.ts`; the submit beside the rest of texting setup in `routes/messaging-setup.ts`; the schemas, fee table, campaign words and marketing check in `packages/core/src/messaging/a2p.ts`. // DECISION |
| Nothing charged twice | A submit never throws once Twilio has made something: a Twilio refusal is kept as an issue and every SID made so far is saved in the same transaction, so a retry carries on (bundles, end users, the address and each assignment, `assigned: ["<bundle>:<object>"]`, are made once) and a brand is registered once. // DECISION |
| A campaign-only resubmit | When only the campaign was rejected, the approved profile, trust product and brand are left alone; the failed campaign is deleted and made again. // DECISION |
| `a2p.status` | `approved` only at `ready`, `failed` at `needs_changes` or `suspended`, `pending` otherwise: today's US-text check (`config.a2p.status === "approved"`) is unchanged. // DECISION |
| Sends | `a2pServiceFor(config, from)`: the Messaging Service when the stage is `ready`, the status `approved` and the sender's PN is in `serviceNumbers`; agents' texts, release-code texts and reply confirmations add `MessagingServiceSid` (with `From`). // DECISION |
| The service's inbound webhook | `UseInboundWebhookOnNumber=true`, so inbound texts keep going to each number's `SmsUrl`, the URL our signature check is made over. // DECISION |
| Repeated fields | `twilioRequest` takes several repeated fields (`repeatedMany`): samples and the three keyword lists go as arrays, one field per value (NOTES.md: the keyword form is UNCHECKED). // DECISION |
| The cron | Its own call in `runCron` (`a2pCronTick`, minute :23), not inside `cronTick`'s per-client pass: it needs the deployment's secrets for the EIN and the subaccount's token. Only `submitted`, `campaign` and `ready` registrations are read. A pass writes a `connection_change` only when something changed. // DECISION |
| Hand-recorded SIDs | Once a registration is submitted by API, `PUT …/messaging/a2p` answers 409 `registered_by_api`; "Check status" runs the API pass for it. // DECISION |
| Portal | A Settings section, `/settings/texting`, for the client admin (client users read "Your admin gives the business details for US texting."). Twilio's industry codes are shown as words, its legal forms and positions as Twilio names them. Status colours: waiting on you (signal) for `draft` and `needs_changes`, ink while Twilio has it, positive when ready, risk when on hold. `needs` lists Twilio's reasons only when changes are needed. // DECISION |
| Back office | The registration panel on the client's messaging page: the details (EIN as its hint), statuses, Twilio's reasons with codes, the campaign's words (editable before a submit), the fees with what this submit charges, a required reason and "I read the fees above" before "Submit to Twilio". // DECISION |

Open for the owner:

- **`TWILIO_PRIMARY_PROFILE_SID`**: the BU SID of the approved "Mezonas" primary customer profile (ISV
  Reseller, Twelve Thirty-Four Consulting LLC), and **`TWILIO_A2P_EMAIL`**, the Mezonas address Trust Hub
  writes to about each client's profile.
- **The monthly fee per use case.** We record $10 (`MIXED`) and $1.50 (`LOW_VOLUME`) a month from TCR's
  rates as resellers quote them; Twilio's help article (1260803965530) is the source to confirm, and
  whether Twilio bills the first three months at once.
- **Campaign edit-in-place.** Asking Twilio support to enable the private beta saves the $15 vetting fee
  on each campaign resubmit (today we delete and recreate).
- **Clients without a privacy policy or terms page.** TCR rejects a campaign without both, on the
  business's own site. Whether Mezonas offers a hosted page for clients is a product choice.
- **Showing the client the fees.** The portal says Twilio's fees are billed as credits but shows no
  amounts (no cost in the portal); whether to show the credits before the operator submits.
- **Edits after approval.** The form is locked once texting is ready; changing an approved brand means a
  brand update and possibly re-vetting.
- **Mock brand check.** Run NOTES.md's mock brand checklist against a real subaccount before the first
  client.

### 1.71 Purchasing end to end by email (P-9, M42; migration 0036; ARCHITECTURE §12.2, §10.8, §10.12)

The owner, 2026-11-08: test a purchasing agent (a "buyer") on staging with email only. The pieces
were built in P-1 to P-6 but didn't connect (the audit of main at d087f7a). This stream joins them:
a person emails a request to the agent's address; the buyer reads it, opens a purchase request and
matches its lines; a person fixes unsure lines on a card; it finds who sold the items and who to
email there; it emails each vendor a request for quote (RFQ), with an optional PDF; a reply as text
or a PDF becomes a recorded quote and a silent vendor expires; it compares the quotes and a person
picks on a card; it makes the PO PDF, emails it, and creates the QuickBooks purchase order with
mapped vendor and line ids, every write behind approval; and the request is ordered. No agent ships:
the owner builds the buyer in the Agent lab (M42's done-when has the steps). Five stacked PRs:
migration 0036 (`claude/p9-purchasing-migration`), the plumbing with this section
(`claude/p9-purchasing-plumbing`), QuickBooks (`claude/p9-purchasing-qbo`), the screens and cards
(`claude/p9-purchasing-ui`) and the proof (`claude/p9-purchasing-e2e`). Hot-spot edits are blocks
headed `// P-9: …`.

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| The message's words | A built-in tool skill `comms.read-message` (handler `inbound.get`) gives a step the message that started the run: who (contact or user id), channel, subject, words, documents and its raw record. The workflow passes the words to `materials.read-request` as its task input (data, invariant 9) and the ids to `materials.open-request`. `read-request`'s `text` is optional: without it the skill calls `inbound.get` itself (in its tools), and the words arrive in that tool's result. // DECISION: a tool skill, not a model's call, so who asked and the raw record reach the request in code. |
| "Not stated" | `materials.request.create` and `materials.quote.record` take null (and an empty string) for any optional field, so a reading skill's output passes straight in. A message's channel `command` or `sloan` is the portal. // DECISION |
| Lines without a quantity | `purchase_request_line.qty` may be null (0036): "some birch" is kept as asked. No vendor is asked, nothing compared and no PO made while a line has none ("line 2 has no quantity yet"). `materials.request.update-lines` changes a line's quantity, unit, words (matched again unless a person chose its item) or note, only while the request is open. // DECISION: once vendors are asked, a change is a new request. |
| Who to email | `materials.vendor-contacts`: vendor → its organization in the directory (`organization.vendor_id`) → its people with an email, consent to email and not opted out, purchasing and sales roles first. The first is the RFQ's `contactId` for reach out's `to`. Nobody: the vendor is listed with the reason and left out of `vendorIds`; an agent never guesses an address. `materials.quote.create` gives each RFQ the same `contactId`. // DECISION |
| The RFQ's words | `quote.create` answers each RFQ's `subject` and `rfqText`, built in code: the vendor's own words for each item (the description on its latest priced invoice line, else its alias), its SKU, quantity and unit, needed-by, delivery or pickup, the reply-by date. Reach out sends it as a fixed message (`message.template.en`), so no model writes to a vendor and a retried step sends the same words. At most 1500 characters; with `withDocument`, lines that don't fit are left to the attached PDF with a line saying how many. // DECISION: English only for now (the vendor's language is open). |
| RFQ PDF | `materials.quote.document` renders the RFQ with the PO's layout titled "Request for quote" (lines, quantities, blank price and lead-time columns, no total), dated the day the RFQ was made, stored as a `generated` document (one per hash), for reach out's `attachments`. // DECISION |
| Sent, from the reach-out | `materials.quote.mark-sent` takes the reach-out step's whole output (`reachOut`): an attempt made → `sent`, `sent_via` from the channel, `reach_out_id` set (0036, a composite foreign key: a quote is tied to the message it answers), `sent_at` the first attempt's time; replied → the reply's words, raw record and first document come back for reading; no reply by the reach-out's timeout → `expired` with the reason, and the request moves on (`quoted` once a quote is in and nothing sent is still waiting); nothing sent (refused, declined, a test run) → the RFQ stays a draft with the reason. `materials.quote.expire` expires what is still waiting (past due, or all). // DECISION |
| Reach out per vendor | Reach out runs inside a `for_each` over `quote.create`'s quotes (concurrently, up to the loop's `concurrency`), one reach-out per vendor (its node id carries the index), each waiting on its own reply. // DECISION: no change to reach out; replies are still matched by the tagged address and only from the contact's own address. |
| PDF quotes | `docs.read` answers a document's reading, reading it first when there is none (the run wakes on the reply before the background reading may have started). `as: quote` (the default) reads it the same way with no totals review and no duplicate check. A reply's attachment (`meta.reachOutReply`) is always read as a quote, by the background too, so a vendor's quote never lands in invoice review. `read-quote` calls `docs.read`. // DECISION: the step reads it rather than wait; a background reading at the same moment may read it twice (a cost, never another answer for the step). |
| News | `materials.request.created` {requestId, channel, lines, unmatched, withoutQuantity}, emitted with the request (a test run's emits nothing). // DECISION |
| The Builder | Told the deployment's packs (it was told none). // DECISION |
| QuickBooks vendor | `materials.vendor.qbo` ties a vendor to its QuickBooks vendor id in `vendor.external_refs.qbo`: the one kept (sync already stores it for vendors and bills with a VendorRef), else the one active QuickBooks vendor of the same name (case, spacing and punctuation aside) among `qbo.vendor.query`'s `vendors`, else the id `qbo.vendor.create` returned (after approval, invariant 8). Two of the same name, or none, is no match: it answers `create`, the input for `qbo.vendor.create`. A different id for a vendor already tied is refused. A test run keeps nothing. // DECISION |
| QuickBooks PO lines | `materials.po.qbo-lines` turns the chosen quote (or a split's `requestLineIds`) into `qbo.purchaseorder.create`'s input as `order`: the vendor's QuickBooks id, one account line per quoted line on the client agent's expense account (skill setting `qbo_account_id`, client-specific, or the step's `accountId`; none: refused), each amount the unit price times the quantity rounded half up to cents in decimal.js, the total their sum, the PO reference as its number, needed-by as its due date when it is not before the order's date. // DECISION: account lines only, because catalog items carry no QuickBooks item id. |
| Split orders | `materials.request.update`'s `order` may name its `quoteRequestId`: a split records one order per vendor in `ext.orders` (the request's columns keep the first), each quote ordered once; without a quote it is ordered once, as before. A split's PO reference adds four characters of its quote's id. // DECISION |
| Import bill prices | A back-office action on the client (`import-bill-prices {from, to}`) runs the bill backfill page after page (`next`), at most 40 pages of 500 bills, as the person (items and prices attributed to them, no agent version). Safe to run again: a bill line is priced once. // DECISION |
| Cards | Two pack review cards beside `materials.match`: the request line review (`review: materials.request-lines`, `requestId`): the request's lines, unsure or without a quantity first, with their candidates and the client's catalog (300 items); a person picks the item (recorded `chosen`) and sets how many, line by line, then Done. The vendor choice (`review: materials.vendor-choice`, `requestId`): the quotes compared as a table (`quote.compare`, in code), the suggestion and its reasons; a person picks one vendor or the suggested split (received quotes of this request only, each line to one vendor), kept on the request as `ext.choice` with who and when; Done answers the choice, and `materials.quote.chosen` gives it to the PO steps. The platform's review decision takes `{action, input}` for any card besides the match review's shape, and the pack's handler checks it. // DECISION |
| Requests screen | The `requests` report is in `PACK_REPORTS` and the manifest's screens: the portal's `/reports/materials/requests` and the back office's `/ops/clients/:id/reports/materials/requests`, then a request's page (`…/requests/:id`) from a new generic route for one row of a pack report (`GET /api/reports/:pack/:report/items/:itemId`, and the back office's under the client): lines (a missing quantity reads "How many?"), quotes and their state, the comparison table, the vendor picked or suggested, the orders, and the RFQ and PO documents (linked to the document pages). Test-run requests are never shown. Design system classes only; the portal's words in six languages (the five other than English need a fluent review). // DECISION |
| Import button | The client page's Packs tab gets "Import bill prices" with a date range, for the Materials pack when it is on. Back office only, English. // DECISION |
| The proof | `apps/api/src/purchasing-email.test.ts`: the buyer built in the test from platform and pack parts, an email to its address through the Worker's email handler, Gmail and QuickBooks fakes and a FakeModel; three RFQs in a `for_each` (Keystone answers with a PDF, Northline in words, Cedar Ridge times out and expires), both cards answered through the portal's review routes, the vendor created in QuickBooks and the PO created, each after one approval, the PO emailed, the request ordered; and the portal's answers checked for forbidden fields. `apps/api/src/converse-email.test.ts`: the first email conversation test. // DECISION |
| Email conversations | A conversation's later emails carry its first message's tag (§1.66), so a reply to any of them came to an ended reach-out and was dropped. Now a reply whose tag names an ended message of a conversation goes to that conversation's message waiting now, from the same contact. // DECISION |
| Not built | Late replies (after a reach-out ended) and replies from a colleague's address are still dropped (§1.65); a vendor that answers that way is entered by a person. RFQs in the vendor's language. The portal channel for conversations (§1.66). |

Open for the owner:

- The RFQ email's wording and language (English, plain, built in code today).
- Whether a vendor with nobody who agreed to email should be asked anyway once the client says so (today it is listed and skipped).
- How long to wait for quotes by default (the reach-out's `timeout`, set per step; we suggest 2 days).
- The QuickBooks account a PO line books to when the item has none (a skill setting per client agent today, §1.71 QuickBooks rows).
### 1.72 The Marketing area: measurement, Mezonas as its own first client (M43; migration 0037; docs/marketing-plan.md)

Owner, 2026-11-08: "build it as a new pack", with Mezonas served as a true client and worked from
the back office. The plan for the whole area (measurement, search, content, the agent set, then
clients) is `docs/marketing-plan.md`; this is M43, the first step. It is outside v0.15's §1 scope
until ARCHITECTURE v0.16; the owner asked for it. Ported from Plenary Labs' marketing portal
(copied, not shared). Hot-spot edits are blocks headed `// M43: …`.

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| A pack | `packs/marketing` (`@countersign/pack-marketing`, id `marketing`), the Marketing area (ARCHITECTURE §12.0). Imports core and db only. Every table has `tenant_id`, forced RLS and the comment `pack:marketing`; one client's figures never reach another (invariant 1). |
| Mezonas as a client | Mezonas is served as its own first client: a normal tenant created from the back office in each environment (`docs/operations.md`, "Mezonas, our own client"), with its approvals delegated to the operator and the pack switched on. Not in the seed: the seed never runs on production, and a third seeded client would change what every seeded test and sweep expects. // DECISION |
| The internal flag | `tenant.settings.internal = true`: one of our own. Its data is real (never purged like a demo); it is never billed, because no Stripe customer is set up for it (the billing pass only reaches clients with one); it gets no Weekly Table. Set by an operator from the client's settings (`PATCH /api/ops/clients/:id/settings {internal}`), logged in `tenant_change` (`setting: internal`). Left out of the command center's and KPIs' client counts later, when their screens show one. // DECISION |
| Tables (0037) | `site` (a client's measured website: domain without `www.`, name, goals, on or off), `site_salt`, `site_event` (90 days), `site_visit` (13 months; times to the hour; `reached_goal` and `goal`, never who), `site_daily_page`, `site_daily_click`, `site_daily_total`, `site_bot_daily`. Composite foreign keys `(site_id, tenant_id)`. The runtime may DELETE on the day and raw tables (a day is rebuilt whole; retention), unlike the platform's history; `site` has no DELETE (a site is switched off). // DECISION |
| Goals, not sign-ups | mezonas.com has no form: a visit's goal is a click on one of the site's goal elements (`data-track` names, e.g. `hero-talk` on "Talk to us"), set per site. A visit says it reached a goal and which, with no link to any person. // DECISION |
| The collector | A generic platform route, `POST /api/in/:pack/:tenantId/:target` (`routes.packInbound`, public): for a registered pack with an `inbound` handler and a client with the pack on, it caps the body at the pack's `maxBytes`, passes the pack only the user agent, origin, language, DNT, Sec-GPC and content type headers, `request.cf` and the IP, and answers 204 with no body to everything, so nothing can be probed. Not `mount()`ed: no session, no cookie read, no audit row (the request is anonymous). A failure is reported without the address or body and still answered 204. Nothing in the platform names the pack (invariant 2). // DECISION |
