| What is kept | The visit is a SHA-256 of the site's salt for the UTC day, the IP and the user agent, cut to 128 bits; the salt is random and deleted once its day is over. Neither the IP nor the user agent is stored or logged. Do Not Track and Global Privacy Control are honoured on the server: such a beacon is not counted at all. |
| Checks on a beacon | The site must be on and the `Origin` must be its domain (or `www.`); then malformed, a bot user agent, `navigator.webdriver`, and more than 500 events from one visit in a day are dropped and counted per reason in `site_bot_daily` ("filtered by heuristics": no Bot Management score). |
| Nightly | A generic pack hook (`RegisteredPack.nightly`, hour and minute): the Marketing pack's runs at 03:41 UTC, per client with the pack on, in that client's transaction: rebuild the last three finished days and any older unbuilt one, then delete events past 90 days, visits past 13 months and every spent salt. |
| Sites | Added and changed by a person from the back office, as pack actions (`add-site`, `update-site`: name, goals, on or off), never by an agent. A refusal (`PackRefusal`) is a 422, as Materials' `MatchError` is. |
| Report | `overview` (portal and back office, JSON and CSV): the last 28 finished days against the 28 before, from the daily totals only: visits, views, goals, bounces, goal and bounce rates, the days, the top pages and sources, and requests filtered; under 20 visits is marked a small sample. |
| No agents yet | The manifest has no tools, skills or templates in M43; the pack will ship the agent set as templates (owner, 2026-11-08), from M47. |
| Not in M43 | The script on mezonas.com, `data-track` names, `/no-measure` and the privacy policy's measurement section (the site's own PR, after the owner and the lawyer read the wording; the site deploys from `main` on every push); the collector's own host (`collect.mezonas.com`, its own PR); the back-office screens (M44). |
| Site: measuring is a build flag | `apps/site/build.mjs` inlines `src/measure.js` (ported from Plenary's `track.js`: view, engaged time, scroll quarter, tracked clicks, a `form[data-form]` seen; `sendBeacon` as `text/plain`; nothing stored in the browser; nothing sent with Do Not Track or GPC) only when the build has `MEZONAS_MEASURE_URL`, the collector's address with the client and site ids (https only, no query; a bad value fails the build). Unset, the output is byte for byte what main built before (checked by `diff -r` when written; `measure.test.ts` holds the two builds together since). The brief's env var stands in for the wave contract's `SITE_MEASURE=on` (docs/marketing-plan.md §11.5): the address is needed anyway, and one variable can't be half set. // DECISION |
| Site: `data-track` names live in the sources | The names are written into `layout.html` and `pages/index.html` and taken out by the build when measuring is off, rather than added by the build when on: one place to read them, and the off output stays identical. Every link and button on the landing page, and the header and footer on every page, has one; unique per page (the build fails otherwise); each listed in `apps/site/README.md` ("Tracked names"), checked both ways. The contact address stays text, not a `mailto:` link: `contact-email` counts a click or tap on it. // DECISION |
| Site: `/no-measure` opts out by DNT or GPC | Built only while measuring (`only: measure`), not measured itself, `noindex`, not in the sitemap, linked from every footer. With no cookies and no storage there is nowhere to keep a choice made on the page, so it has no switch: the opt-out is Do Not Track or Global Privacy Control, and the page says so plainly (the lawyer's question 3 in the draft). // DECISION |
| Site: the policy comes first | The privacy policy's measurement section is a draft, `docs/marketing/privacy-measurement-draft.md`, for the owner and the lawyer; the live `/privacy` is unchanged. A measuring build is refused while `/privacy` says "This website uses no analytics" or does not link `/no-measure`, so the policy and the script go live together. // DECISION |
| Site: the Worker's first code serves guides | `apps/site/src/worker.ts` (entry; a Worker's main module may export only handlers) and `src/guides.ts` (ported from Plenary's `guides.ts`, `index.ts`, `chrome.ts`). `run_worker_first` only for `/guides`, `/guides/*` and `/sitemap.xml`; everything else is static as before. Guides are on only with the `SITE_CONTENT` R2 binding **and** `SITE_GUIDES=on` (docs/marketing-plan.md §11.5); otherwise those paths go to the assets unchanged (the 404 page, the static sitemap), so deploying the code changes nothing for visitors. Neither is in `wrangler.jsonc` (owner). No runtime dependency, no Workers types package: the few binding types are written out. // DECISION |
| Site: a guide's layout is the built 404 page | At serve time the Worker fetches the built `/404` and replaces its head (title, description, canonical, Open Graph, no `noindex`, Article data) and main content, rather than keeping a copy of the header and footer in code (Plenary's `chrome.ts`): `layout.html` stays the one source, and a guide carries the measuring script exactly when the build has it. A layout the Worker can't recognise answers 503, never a half-wrapped page. A guide's own `<h1>`, if its body has one, is the page's heading. A body with active content (scripts, frames, forms, styles, event handlers, `javascript:` or `data:` addresses) is refused as missing. Redirects (`redirects.json`) apply only to `/guides/*` and only to our own paths. If R2 fails, a guide is a 503 and the sitemap the static one. // DECISION |

---

## 2. Package layering

Arrows point to allowed dependencies. Each package's `package.json` lists only these, and
`scripts/check-deps.mjs` (run by `pnpm lint:deps`) fails on any other workspace import, on a platform
package that depends on a pack, and on a pack that depends on anything outside its allowed set.

```
apps/api ─▶ workflows ─▶ agents ─▶ documents ─▶ connectors ─▶ db ─▶ core
   │            │          └─▶ threads ─────────────────────▶ db
   │            │          └─▶ llm, senses, connectors
   │            └─▶ senses ─▶ db
   ├─▶ auth ─▶ db
   └─▶ packs/materials ─▶ documents, connectors, llm, senses, db, core   (never agents or workflows)
apps/web ─▶ core                    (route contract, schemas, workflow validation; HTTP to apps/api)
apps/mfa ─▶ core                    (the code service, §1.26: its own Worker; apps/api reaches it only by service binding)
apps/site ─▶ design-system          (the public site, §1.29: static files only)
```

| Package | Workspace deps |
|---|---|
| `core` | none |
| `db` | core |
| `llm` | core |
| `auth` | db, core |
| `senses` | db, core |
| `connectors` | db, core |
| `documents` | connectors, llm, senses, db, core |
| `threads` | senses, db, core |
| `agents` | documents, threads, connectors, llm, senses, db\*, core |
| `workflows` | agents, threads, senses, db, core |
| `packs/materials` | documents, connectors, llm, senses, db, core |
| `apps/api` | any |
| `apps/web` | core |
| `apps/mfa` | core |
| `apps/site` | design-system (files only) |

\* only from `agents/src/registry/` and `agents/src/log/` (see §1.2).

The registries in `agents` hold tools and skills from three sources: the platform, connectors and packs.
`apps/api` builds them at startup from the platform's own list, each registered connector's manifest,
and each pack's manifest; nothing in `packages/*` names a pack.

---

## 3. Build order: the platform first

The **platform is the product** (owner, 2026-11-03). Use cases (Pia and the Materials pack, invoice
intake, collections) and the tools they need (QuickBooks, email forwarding, Gmail, documents
extraction, …) are built on it afterwards. Cloud setup (Neon, Cloudflare, WorkOS) comes after the
foundation.

**Phase 1: Foundation (now, local only).** Everything that every agent, tool and client will rely on,
tied together and tested against local Postgres and `wrangler dev`, with no cloud accounts and no
domain tools:
- toolchain, app and Worker scaffolds, the two UI shells with Groundwork tokens (M0 without the deploy
  spike)
- `packages/core`: every schema (bundle, agent, skill, workflow steps and rules, approvals, tools,
  connectors, packs, events), workflow validation and template typing, rules → JSONLogic, bundle diff
  and three-way merge, canonical hashing, the route contract and roles (M1 core)
- `packages/db`: the platform schema, roles, RLS on `app.tenant_ids` / `app.operator`, immutability
  triggers, migrations, `withTenant(s)`, seed (operator, Sandbox client, platform templates) (M1 db)
- `packages/llm`, `packages/auth` (dev + WorkOS adapters), `packages/senses`
- `packages/connectors`: the connector framework, permission matrix, credential encryption, object
  storage, and a test connector with a read and a write tool (M2 framework; no real connectors)
- `packages/agents`: registries, bundle versions, fork, publish, rollback, the runtime, the permission
  gate with per-step approvals, external actions, logging and usage (M3)
- `packages/workflows`: the step-list executor, trigger router and test runs (M4)
- `apps/api`: Hono routes for the foundation, middleware, `RunWorkflow`, cron; `apps/web`: back office
  and portal shells wired to the API (M5 shells)

Tested against a fake Google token endpoint and Gmail send endpoint. Open: `gmail/NOTES.md` "To verify", the owner's consent screen, deploy secrets and Google's verification |

**Phase 2: Cloud.** Bootstrap workflow, deploy spike, staging (M0's deploy spike, `docs/operations.md`).

**Phase 3: Product surfaces and tools, as needed.** Track B built most of the M5–M7 screens (inbox,
portal connections, templates and client agents, the builder, run views; progress above). Left: reports and usage screens; then
QuickBooks (M9) and Gmail (M10, now in the MVP), approver rules and Stripe billing (§1.17), languages
(§1.18); then texts and calls (§1.19). Pia and the Materials pack (M8) are built separately
(ARCHITECTURE v0.9).

---

## 4. Milestones

Each milestone lists the work, the tests it adds, and the check that closes it.

### M0: Monorepo, Worker, app, DB, Workflows, auth

Work
- Deploy spike (do this first, against real Cloudflare + Neon, on the resources the Bootstrap cloud workflow created): Worker `countersign-staging` with WorkOS sign-in; Hyperdrive → Neon `staging` with one `withTenant()` round trip (checks `set_config` stays inside the transaction); R2 put, get and a 5-minute signed URL; a `HelloWorkflow` instance that runs one `step.do` making a 60–90 s Claude call, waits on `step.waitForEvent`, resumes on `sendEvent`, and on a second instance takes the timeout path. Any surprise here changes this plan before M1.
- `apps/api`: Hono Worker. `wrangler.jsonc` with assets (`../web/dist`), Hyperdrive, R2 and Workflow bindings, cron `* * * * *`. `/api/health`, `/api/session`. Zod route contract in `packages/core/src/api/`.
- `apps/web`: Vite + React + React Router, Tailwind + shadcn primitives, Groundwork tokens copied from `docs/design/tokens.css`, fonts per §1.2. Two shells (header with client switcher and command-bar placeholder, sidebar as in `docs/design/screens/1-home.png`); the shells (§1.2): back office (`/ops`) and portal, with the ARCHITECTURE §15 screens as placeholders. Typed client from the route contract.
- `packages/db`: Drizzle client (`postgres-js` over Hyperdrive), `drizzle.config.ts` (platform + pack schemas), migration runner (direct URL), `withTenant()`. Migration `0000_platform`: extensions (`vector`, `pg_trgm`, `pgcrypto`), `uuid_generate_v7()`, the `cs_runtime` role and its grants, plus the `tenant`, `app_user` and `membership` tables (M1 adds the rest).
- `packages/auth`: `getSession()`, `requireRole()`, `activeTenant()`. `dev` and `workos` adapters (ported from Plenary Labs' `apps/api/src/identity/`).
- Seed: the operator (`operator@example.com`), clients "Sandbox" and "Millwork demo", one client_admin for the millwork client.
- `packages/db/scripts/provision-worker-role.mjs`: creates or re-keys `cs_worker` (used by `scripts/setup-postgres.sh` and on each Neon branch). Port Plenary Labs' `scripts/connect.sh` to wire Hyperdrive and local env files.
- Already done in repo setup: toolchain, Biome, `scripts/check-deps.mjs` (including the pack rules), `pnpm dev` / `dev:web` scripts, `ci.yml` / `checks.yml` / `deploy.yml`, `scripts/setup-postgres.sh`. M0 adds `wrangler`, `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers`, Vite and React, and the `staging` env in `apps/api/wrangler.jsonc`.

Tests: `withTenant` sets and clears the setting; check-deps fails on a bad import (fixture); `HelloWorkflow` resumes on an event and times out without one (Workers pool).

**Done when** `pnpm dev` runs the Worker (API + Workflows) and the app, signing in as the operator lands on the back office shell and as the client admin on the portal shell, and the deployed spike signs in through WorkOS, reads a tenant row from Neon and resumes a waiting Workflow.

### M1: Platform schema, core schemas, workflow validation, merge, seed

Work
- Drizzle schema for ARCHITECTURE §5.1–5.3: connections and raw data, `external_action`, shared business records, `tenant_pack`, skills, agents (templates and client agents) with bundle versions and lineage, `agent_change`, runs, events, threads, inbox, memory (per client agent), `usage_daily`.
- Custom migrations: RLS loop over every tenant table (`app.tenant_ids` policy; for `skill`, `skill_version`, `agent`, `agent_version`, `agent_change` also `tenant_id is null and app.operator`), `forbid_update_except` triggers (§1.2), grants to `cs_runtime`.
- `packages/core`: Zod schemas `AgentBundle`, `AgentDefinition` v2, `SkillDefinition`, `Workflow` + step kinds and rules, `ToolSpec`, `ConnectorManifest`, `PackManifest`, `EventTypeSpec`, each also exported as JSON Schema. **Workflow validation** (ARCHITECTURE §10.2) returning errors with node id and field, the template resolver + typer, and **`diffBundles` / `mergeBundles`** (§1.2). Units table and conversions. Money.
- Seed: operator, Sandbox and Millwork clients with users, platform templates (`document-clerk`, `coordinator`, `builder`) as v1 rows, Sandbox fixture data. `bundle_hash` = sha256 of canonical JSON (sorted keys).

Tests
- Migrations apply on an empty DB, roll back, re-apply; the seed runs twice without duplicates.
- RLS: as `cs_worker`, a session for client A sees nothing of client B on every tenant table (one test loops over the table list); an operator session for {A, B} sees both and nothing of C; no ids set → no rows. Privileges: `cs_worker` is not a member of `neon_superuser` and can't bypass RLS.
- Immutability: `UPDATE agent_version SET bundle…` fails; `UPDATE … SET status` works; `DELETE` fails. Same for the other version tables, `raw_record` and `doc_extraction`.
- Merge (may land with the update work after the MVP): golden cases (ARCHITECTURE §19) including a conflict per kind; merge never regenerates ids; fork → edit → merge → rollback round trip.
- Workflow validation: one failing fixture per rule, plus valid fixtures for every step kind; template typing across fork paths (a field produced inside a path is unavailable after the join); rules compile to the expected JSONLogic.

**Done when** migrations apply, RLS holds for client and operator sessions (templates invisible to client sessions), the seed inserts the operator, clients and platform templates, and the validation and merge suites pass.

### M2: Connector framework, upload, documents

Work
- `packages/connectors`: `Connector` interface and manifest (ARCHITECTURE §6.1), connector registry, `ObjectStore` (R2 binding; local adapter for tests). `upload/`: files go to `document(source='upload')`, stored once per sha256; **email forwarding** (§1.2): the `email()` handler, per-client addresses, allowed senders, attachments to `document(source='email')`; CSV of purchases goes to `raw_record(connector='upload')`, then `mapUploadPurchase` produces transactions.
- `packages/core/mappers`: raw → `TransactionInput` (upload now, QBO in M9).
- `packages/llm`: client wrapper, record/replay, cost accounting, `callTool<T>(schema)`.
- `packages/documents`: store, `findSupport(transactionId)`, `extract(documentId)` → `doc_extraction` + `doc_line` (always a new row), `validateTotals()` (ARCHITECTURE §7), `linkToTransaction()` by vendor + total + date ±7 days. Platform tools `docs.*` and skills `docs.find-support`, `docs.extract`.
- `POST /api/uploads`, `GET /api/documents/:id` (5-minute signed URL + latest extraction), `GET /api/connectors`.
- Fixtures: 3 synthetic millwork invoices (sheet goods, hardwood, hardware/finishes; one with freight + fuel surcharge; one multi-page) plus `*.expected.json`. Recorded LLM responses.

Tests: forwarding accepts only allowed senders, stores attachments once, keeps no bodies (fixture `.eml` files); totals validation (tolerance edges); golden extraction in replay mode (header exact; each line: qty and prices within $0.01, description fuzzy ≥ 0.9); dedupe by sha256; linking window; manifests validate.

**Done when** uploading the 3 fixture invoices produces extractions that match the expected JSON within tolerance.

### M3: Registries, agent runtime, permission gate, external actions

Work in `packages/agents`.
- `registry/`: tool registry (platform + connector + pack sources), skill registry (built-ins from code, operator-written skills from `skill_version` rows, shared or per client), agent registry (resolve a client agent's live or given version; availability per client; `createVersion` rejecting a no-op by hash; `publish` in one transaction; `rollback` as a copy), fork (template version → client agent v1, ids kept), and the operational fields with `agent_change`.
- `runtime/`: `runAgent()` per ARCHITECTURE §9.5: tool-mode skills call their handler; agent-mode skills run the loop with the skill's tools and a `finish` tool typed to the skill output; budgets → `escalated` + inbox item; `sources` required for skills that produce alerts. Document text only ever in tool results (§16).
- Permission gate and **external actions** (§1.2): `none` runs; `internal` runs if allowed, else approval; `external` needs approval unless the step says `not_required`, the connection allows `auto` and the agent's mode is `act` (§1.2 Step approvals); `off` is refused.
- `log/`: `step_run` (with `summary` from the skill's template), `tool_call`, `external_action` rows with tokens and cost; `usage_daily` rollup.
- A test-only connector (`testkit`) with one read tool and one external write tool, so external actions are tested without QBO.

Tests: version publish/rollback/no-op for agents and skills; the gate gives each verb × connection setting × step approval × agent mode the right outcome (approval whenever any of them says so); a client tightening the matrix overrides a step at once; an approved external action executes exactly once across a retried step; a rejected one never executes; budget hit escalates; a test agent with `docs.extract` in replay mode.

**Done when** a test template forked into the Sandbox client runs `docs.extract` end to end and logs cost, and its external tool call creates an approval and executes once when approved.

### M4: Workflow executor, triggers, senses, test runs

Work
- `packages/senses`: typed `emit()` (outbox), `undispatched()`, `markDispatched()`. Emitted by mappers, documents, inbox and external actions.
- `packages/workflows`: trigger router over the live versions of active client agents, `StepRunner` interface, executors for every step kind, fork path order and `for_each` (ARCHITECTURE §10.1, §10.3); each step writes `step_run`; cost rolls up to `run`; every run appends to its subject's thread.
- `apps/api`: `RunWorkflow` class, after-commit dispatch in `ctx.waitUntil`, cron sweep and schedule check, manual and command triggers. **Test runs** (`mode='test'`): external actions recorded as proposed only, no events emitted.
- Event catalogue (`GET /api/catalog/events`): platform + enabled pack event types with sample payloads.

Tests: template resolution; JSONLogic conditions; an event for client A starts only A's agents; a paused client agent starts nothing; a template never runs except as a test; schedules fire in the client's timezone; executor with a fake `StepRunner` for every step kind, first-match fork paths, otherwise, for_each with concurrency, a wait that resolves and one that times out; `RunWorkflow` in the Workers pool for one full run; a re-dispatched event doesn't start a second run; the cron sweep picks up an undispatched event; a test run never executes an external action.

**Done when** a workflow with a fork, a for_each and a person approval runs to the end for one client's agent and never starts for another client, and a test run proposes but never executes external actions.

### M5: Back office and portal shells, onboarding, inbox, requests

Work
- **Back office** (`/ops`): overview (needs attention across clients: failed runs, operator inbox, open requests, connections a client needs to reconnect, template updates waiting); clients list with health; new client (create, invite the client admin through WorkOS, offer connectors, packs, approval delegation); client detail shell with connectors offered, their health, permission matrix and configuration; operator inbox; requests queue.
- **Portal**: overview shell (services, what needs you); inbox (approval, external action with tool / target record / now and after this change, request, extraction review); **connections** (owner's design, screen 2): offered tools, sign in (provider OAuth), reconnect, disconnect, what agents may do in each tool (view; tighten only), used by, upload; requests (raise, see status); settings (users, notifications).
- `POST /api/inbox/:id/resolve` (and the `/ops` twin) → resolution + `inbox.resolved` + `sendEvent` to the waiting run. Approver from the client agent, else the client setting; every approval records who.
- Role checks and both contract tests (§1.2). Threads basics with `audience` (operator-only notes never in the portal).

Tests: resolving an approval resumes the waiting run (Workers pool); client_user sees only items assigned to them; a client admin can tighten but not loosen a permission matrix; only offered connectors can be connected; operator roles can't call the client's authorize route; the contract tests; an operator sees only the clients they serve.

**Done when** the operator creates a client, offers it the upload connector and invites its admin; the admin signs in to the portal, sees the offered connectors and their permissions, approves an item that resumes a run, and raises a request the operator sees in the queue.

Status (2026-11-04): onboarding and settings are built (§1.14): the overview, new client with the admin's invite, client settings, portal settings. The requests queue is its own stream; the done-when walk needs it.

### M6: Templates and client agents

Work
- **Templates** (`/ops/templates`): list; new (from scratch: name, role, mission, instructions, model; or describe → Builder); editor (identity, instructions, skills with settings, extra tools, permissions with the external-never-auto rule shown, budgets); versions with change and release notes, diffs, publish, rollback; "Used by" (each client agent: base version, versions behind, customizations, conflicts); skills library with the operator skill editor (ARCHITECTURE §9.2); packs.
- **Client agents** (`/ops/clients/[id]/agents`): fork a template (version, slug, service name), or create from scratch, or copy another client's agent; the same editor; customizations view (diff vs base template version); versions, publish, rollback; bindings, approver, test run on the client's data, activate, pause, retire; detach and re-link.
- **Updates and promotion**: update preview (merged result, conflicts), resolutions in a table view (step conflicts get the builder's merge view in M7), merge draft, test run, publish; promote selected changes into a template draft.
- Builder: `draft-agent` (template, or client agent with `clientId`) and `revise-agent` (ARCHITECTURE §9.6); server guards; drafts open in the editor and the canvas.
- Portal: services list and service page (name, description, status, recent runs) from active client agents.

Tests: guards hold even when the model output violates them (external tool in `auto`, unknown skill, invalid workflow); fork keeps ids and base version; activation refuses missing bindings or no passing test run; a template publish changes no client agent; update with and without conflicts; promotion strips client-specific values; rollback per client; client roles get 403 on every `/api/ops` route; the Builder script below in replay mode.

**Done when** the operator builds a template, forks it into two clients and customizes each differently, publishes template v2 and merges it into both (one clean, one with a resolved conflict) keeping each client's customizations, promotes one client's change into template v3, and rolls one client back; "flag card charges over $500 missing receipts" produces a valid draft with gaps listed (no card connector); each client sees only its own services in the portal.

### M7: Workflow builder, merge view and run view

Work (`apps/web`, ARCHITECTURE §10.5, owner's design screens 4–7)
- Builder: header (name, version badge, counts of steps, forks, actions and approvals; Test run; Publish; tabs); add-a-step palette (triggers from the event catalogue, the agent's skills then the library, actions grouped by tool, people, logic); numbered step list with drag handles, forks as side-by-side paths with "Drop a step here" and "paths join here", `for_each` blocks; delete, undo/redo, keyboard reordering.
- Inspector: forms generated from skill/tool input schemas; template field picker of upstream outputs valid at the step; fork path rule rows (+ Add condition, + Add path, remove fork); settings with defaults, and on a client agent the template's value beside a customized one; client timeline label and visibility; for steps that change a tool, the **Approval** control (on by default; "run without approval" offered only where the client's connection allows it) with the approver, and badges for which steps will wait and which run automatically.
- Live validation (shared `packages/core` code) with step badges and a clickable summary; save draft; test run with a chosen payload (sample event, recent record, manual input); publish; versions with a step-by-step diff and rollback; "Based on template vN" / "Used by"; updates and promotion.
- **Merge view**: the step list with each step marked added, removed, changed or moved per side; conflicts resolved per step or field; validation on the merged workflow.
- **Run view** (screen 6): what it did as a checklist with summaries, cost and links; changes to your tools as now / after this change with approve, reject, edit lines; full log.
- New agent flow (screen 4): describe → Builder's suggested steps → open in builder; or blank; or a template.
- Portal run timeline (ARCHITECTURE §10.6): client-visible steps with label, status, time, summary, sources and approvals; no costs, instructions or internal steps.

Tests: component tests for add, drag to reorder, move into and out of a fork path, delete, undo, invalid markers; a round trip (builder → workflow JSON → builder) keeps every step, path and id; the done-when script as a Playwright test against `pnpm dev`.

**Done when** the operator customizes a client's workflow in the builder (adds a step, adds a fork path with two rules), test-runs it on that client's data and publishes; resolves a conflict from a template update in the merge view; and watches the live run in the run view while the client sees the timeline without internals or cost.

### M8: Materials pack and Pia

Work in `packs/materials` (ARCHITECTURE §12.2–12.4).
- Schema (`catalog_item`, `item_alias`, `item_match`, `price_observation`) in the shared migration sequence, with RLS and grants; the `item_alias` expression unique index.
- Catalog: `normalize()`, parsers (sheet goods, hardwood, hardware required; mouldings/edgebanding and finishes/adhesives basic + LLM fallback), each declaring **key attributes**; alias lookup → candidates (equal key attributes, then `pg_trgm`) → Haiku adjudication → route by `match_confidence_auto` → hard rules as a veto; alias learning on human decisions; merge.
- Pricing: `record()` (std unit price; freight not folded in; landed cost separate); the 6 alert rules with thresholds from skill settings; `dollar_impact`; one open alert per (kind, item, vendor).
- Manifest: tools, skills (with summary templates and `clientSpecific` settings such as vendor lists), events, the Pia template with both workflows (validated by the same workflow validation); enabling the pack for the millwork client, forking Pia into it and customizing her (its vendors, categories, thresholds, approvers).
- Screens under `apps/web/src/packs/materials/`: portal reports Price watch (item × vendor table with last price, Δ%, 90-day sparkline; alerts with evidence links) and Catalog, with CSV export; the same views for any client in the back office, plus merge; match review card in the Inbox. Portal activity report (runs, documents processed, actions taken, alerts and dollar impact for the period).
- Fixtures: `fixtures/matching/*.json` (≥ 150 labeled pairs, heavy on near-misses: 3/4 vs 1/2, BB/BB vs B/BB, 4x8 vs 5x5, prefinished vs not); seed script `seed:demo-prices` (6 months, ~10 items, 3 vendors).

Tests: parser tables (`3/4"`, `.75`, `18MM`, `23/32` → `3/4in`; `4X8`, `48X96`, `4'X8'` → `48x96in`); golden precision ≥ 95% on "same" at the auto threshold, recall reported; Baltic birch across 3 vendors → one item; one table-driven test per alert rule at the threshold edge; dedupe; UOM guard; the pack's templates pass graph validation; layering (the pack imports no `agents`/`workflows`).

**Done when** golden tests pass, Baltic birch matches across 3 vendors, an invoice uploaded by the millwork client runs its own Pia's purchase-intake to priced lines with a `price_jump` alert, and the client sees it in Price watch.

### M9: QuickBooks Online

First, check against Intuit's current developer docs: base URLs, minor version, CDC limits, webhook signature header and payload format, Attachable download and upload, sparse updates and `SyncToken` rules, throttling limits and the current API terms. Record the findings in `packages/connectors/src/qbo/NOTES.md`.

Work: OAuth from the portal's Connections page (authorize, callback, encrypted tokens with AES-256-GCM, refresh and persist the new refresh token every time); initial sync with paging; CDC every 15 min plus a webhook nudge (`POST /api/webhooks/qbo` verifies the HMAC and starts a `QboSyncWorkflow` instance for the realm); attachables fetched, stored once per sha256 and linked; per-realm throttle with backoff on 429; QBO mappers including both line detail types; read tools; the MVP write tools `qbo.bill.update_lines` / `qbo.purchase.update_lines` (refused if the total would change) and `qbo.attachable.upload`, approval on by default, with `before` capture and stale-`SyncToken` handling.

Tests: mappers on `fixtures/qbo/*`; webhook signature; token refresh rotation; sync is idempotent per `(connection, entity, id, SyncToken)`; a write with a stale token fails cleanly and leaves the action `failed` with the reason.

**Done when** a sandbox company syncs, a Bill flows through intake, and approving in the portal itemizes its lines (total unchanged) and attaches the invoice in the sandbox. **MVP exit** (§3) follows on the shop's production QuickBooks.

Status (2026-11-05): built against a fake QuickBooks (§1.30). The done-when holds against the fake: a company syncs, a new bill raises `transaction.created` and starts an intake run, and approving in the portal itemizes its lines (total unchanged) and attaches the invoice, once. The live sandbox check and the docs check are listed in `packages/connectors/src/qbo/NOTES.md`; also, the minor version, the webhook format and the throttling limits were not checked first, as this milestone asked, because Intuit's docs could not be reached.

### M10: Gmail (send only; owner, 2026-11-05)

No restricted scopes, so no CASA (ARCHITECTURE §6.4). Reading mail stays with forwarding (§1.6, built).

Work: OAuth with scope `gmail.send` only (authorize, callback, tokens in the vault, refresh, disconnect revokes); the `gmail.send` tool behind approval, used by reach out (§1.25) as an email channel; replies arrive through the client's forwarding address. Google's standard review: the public site's homepage and privacy policy (§1.29), domain verification (done 2026-11-05), a demo video, the scope's reason. Record what Google's current docs say about `gmail.send` (classification, review, quotas) in `packages/connectors/src/gmail/NOTES.md`.

Tests: the send tool only runs after approval and runs once (idempotency key); the token never reaches a response; disconnect revokes.

**Done when** a test mailbox connects with `gmail.send` only, an approved reach-out email is sent from it, and its reply arrives through forwarding.

### M11: Usage, reports, notifications, end-to-end

Work: back-office usage and cost (per client, client agent and template, by month; `usage_daily`); portal overview and activity report polish; notification emails to clients (weekly digest, items waiting); every alert and external action links to its run; empty states and first-run guidance for the operator (create client → invite → connect links → enable packs → deploy) and for clients (connect your tools → your inbox).

Tests: Playwright e2e: the operator creates a client, forks Pia into it and customizes her → the client admin signs in to its tools in the portal and uploads an invoice → approves in the portal → sees the activity and Price watch reports; the operator publishes a Pia template update and merges it into that client; the operator sees the runs and their cost (ARCHITECTURE §19).

**Done when** the e2e passes against `pnpm dev`.

Status (stream M11, 2026-11-04): usage and reports built (§1.15): the rollup (documents, every day a run touched, the hourly sweep), `/api/ops/usage` and `/ops/usage`, `/api/reports/activity` and the portal's `/reports` with a slot for pack reports, `/api/services/:id` and the Service page. Not yet: notification emails, links from every alert and external action to its run, first-run guidance.

Status (e2e, 2026-11-06): the e2e exists at the API level, `apps/api/src/e2e.test.ts`, in the normal `pnpm test` (DB-backed). It walks the managed-service path through the real app against Postgres, with fake QuickBooks, Stripe and model, using the seeded Bill clerk rather than Pia (the Materials pack is built separately): the operator creates the client with its admin invited, offers QuickBooks and upload, installs and forks the template; the client admin connects QuickBooks and the first sync runs; a first invoice arrives while the agent is off; the operator binds, publishes, test-runs on that invoice and activates; the Northline invoice is uploaded, the cron's sweep starts the run, the client admin signs three changes in the portal inbox and each is written once; the portal's runs, timeline, activity report, service page and records, the back office's runs with cost, usage and billing's daily record show it; a template update is merged into the client agent and test-run; a second client sees none of it and no portal response carries prompts, bundles or cost. The Playwright e2e through the UI waits for the owner's redesign.

Status (W1-H platform fixes, 2026-11-06): the four gaps the e2e found are fixed (§1 "Platform fixes (W1-H)"): the upload's run starts without the sweep, background reading shows in usage and credits, the portal lists records (`GET /api/records`, `/records`), and "sync now" right after connecting queues a second sync that runs when the first ends. The e2e checks each.

### M12: Access (vault, grants, code service, browser connections)

ARCHITECTURE v0.10, §1.26. Phase 1: `tenant_key`, envelope v2 and the hourly re-encryption; `agent_grant`
and the effective verb; `credentialFields` and the write-only forms; the portal's `/access` and the back
office's grants. Phase 2: `apps/mfa` (identities, waits, claim once, SMS and email intake, authenticator
codes, caps, alerts) and the `CODES` binding. Phase 3: `browser_site` and its versions, browser connections,
the `BrowserDriver`, sign-in and sign-out by code, `browser.page.read` and `browser.file.download`, caps and
stops.

Done when: an agent with a read-only grant signs in to a low-tier fixture site as its agent account, with
an emailed code from the code service, downloads an invoice as a document and signs out; the model's
transcript contains neither the password nor the code; a second agent without the binding is refused;
a code that arrives with no wait open raises an alert and is never handed out.


### M13–M29: the owner's prototype (ARCHITECTURE v0.12)

One milestone per build stream (§8). Each lists its decisions section, work, tests and the check that
closes it. Every milestone also keeps `pnpm check` green, adds contract specs for its routes (the role
and forbidden-field tests then cover them), and extends `apps/api/src/e2e.test.ts` where its change
touches that path.

### M13: Go-live stages and client controls (W1-A, §1.36)

Work: `agent.stage` and its transitions; `effectiveMode` at the gate, reach out and call answering;
the hourly trial → signoff pass and the client's sign-off item; stage skips with reasons; client pause,
pause all, resume, `until`; Run now with forms; running live on a past event.

Tests: each stage × step `not_required` × matrix `auto` × mode `act` (only live runs without a
signature); trial minimums both needed; skip forward needs a reason, back doesn't; the backfill sets
live agents to `live`; a client can't resume an operator's pause; `until` resumes on the cron; Run now
refuses an invalid form and a viewer; a past event runs once (409 the second time) and never from a
test run's event.

**Done when** an agent goes bench → trial (a step set to run without approval still waits) → signoff
(minimums lowered in the test) → live on the client admin's approval; the client admin pauses all
with a reason and resumes; Run now starts a client-runnable workflow; and the API e2e's first invoice,
which arrived while the agent was off, runs live once from the back office.

### M14: Notifications, the Table and the Weekly Table (W1-B, §1.37)

Work: the `notification` outbox and its 5-minute pass; the mailer over Resend and texts through the
client's number; per-person preferences; `/remind`; `GET /api/table`; the Weekly Table's draft, lead
edits, Monday send and PDF.

Tests: one items-waiting email an hour per person (a second pass sends nothing); quiet hours hold texts;
a viewer gets no items-waiting; no message carries a record field (a walk over every rendered message);
the Weekly Table sends at 7:00 in two time zones; a sent table can't be edited; the PDF parses and
holds the stats; with no key, rows are `skipped`.

**Done when** items waiting, a connection in error and a weekly table each produce one notification
with a count and a link (against the fake Resend), and the Weekly Table drafts, takes the lead's note,
sends on Monday 7:00 in the client's zone and downloads as a PDF.

### M15: Threads, the lead and `/commands` (W1-C, §1.38)

Work: `packages/threads` (store, access, read state, commands); thread routes for both surfaces; the
lead setting and session field; lead checks; `threads.post` and `threads.read`; `thread.message_posted`.

Tests: who reads and posts each kind (a client user can't read another person's account thread; a
second client sees nothing); one lead thread per client under concurrency; sources from another client
refused; `/remy` resolves to the agent, `/nobody` answers with a system message; a lead check lands on
the lead's user, or the operator role without a lead.

**Done when** a client member and the lead talk in the lead thread with unread counts, a workflow's lead
check is assigned to the lead, and `/remy hello` posts a command message and emits the event.

### M16: The Desk, sign-off and viewers (W1-D, §1.39)

Work: `whyApproval`; choices; snooze; edits before signing; batches; the undo window and its delivery;
`can_sign`, `/api/team`.

Tests: the undo window end to end in the Workers pool (undone: never written; not undone: written once
after the window, run resumed); a check before waiting inside the window doesn't take the resolution;
an edit to a fixed input or guard refused, a valid edit executed with the agent's input kept; a reroute
refused; a batch of three with two approved and one rejected; a viewer can't resolve and can't be named
in a rule; snooze capped by the due time.

**Done when** a question is answered with one tap, an item is snoozed, an email's subject is edited
before signing and sent once with the edit (both versions shown), 2 of 3 are signed, an undone
approval writes nothing, and a viewer can't sign.

### M17: Run controls and model calls (W1-E, §1.40)

Tests: a retry reuses earlier outputs and executes a carried approved action under its original key
exactly once; an approval older than 72 hours or a tightened matrix asks again; cancel expires items and
rejects proposals, waits for an executing write; replay writes nothing; model-call bodies are redacted
and never reachable from the portal; saved views are per user.

**Done when** a run that failed at its send step (the prototype's Gmail 401) is retried from that step
and sends the already-signed emails once without asking again; a waiting run is cancelled; failures
group by error; and the run page lists its model calls.

Tests: mappers on fixtures; balances and `paid` from payments' effect on invoices; no events for
history; the note write changes only `PrivateNote` (the fake checks the sparse body) and refuses a stale
SyncToken; create refuses a duplicate and a total that doesn't add up, and is created once across a

**Done when** a fake company's invoices, customers and payments sync, the aging report reads, a note
lands on one invoice's private note only, and a bill is created once after approval.

vendors import as a preview, and a forwarded `+voice` email becomes a sample the skill reads as data.

credits, the portal lists records, and "sync now" after connecting syncs again.

Receivables join the portal list with W1-F.

with one decision row and one proposal for the operator; a write that reached the client's tool is
never retried; the fourth intervention in a run fails the step as before; the portal shows only

call on a clean run; `ask` waits and resumes on approval; `stop` at a checkpoint cancels the run.

**Done when** a WhatsApp photo from a contact whose conversation has attachments on becomes one
document, extracted, linked to its message; with it off, only the attachment's existence is kept; a

wait; the step output carrying the ids; the portal's answers with no link, bundle or cost.

**Done when** a reach-out emails a purchase order PDF from the step's input after one approval

and linked to the reply, and the next step gets its id; the same works by text and WhatsApp; a
document of another client is never sent.

**Done when**, on staging, a buyer built in the Agent lab takes an email to its address to a
QuickBooks purchase order: the request is opened and its lines matched, an unsure line answered on
the card, two vendors emailed an RFQ, one reply recorded from a PDF, a silent vendor expired, the
vendor picked on the card, the PO PDF emailed and the QuickBooks PO created, each write approved
once, and the client and the back office see the request ordered.
### M43: The Marketing area, measurement (§1.72; docs/marketing-plan.md)

PRs: the migration (0037) on its own; the pack, the collector route, the nightly hook and the
internal flag; then, after the owner's review of the wording, the site's script and privacy section;
and the collector's host.

- Migration: `site`, `site_salt`, `site_event`, `site_visit`, `site_daily_page`, `site_daily_click`,
  `site_daily_total`, `site_bot_daily`.
- Work: `packs/marketing` (measure: the beacon, sources, the visit hash, the store, the nightly
  build and retention; sites; the overview report); `routes.packInbound` and
  `apps/api/src/routes/pack-inbound.ts`; `RegisteredPack.inbound` and `nightly`; the internal flag.

Tests (throwaway Postgres, as the Worker's role): a view from the site's own page stored with its
source and place and no address; another origin, a switched-off site, DNT, a bot user agent,
webdriver and a malformed body each dropped and counted; a day built into visits and totals with
its goal and times to the hour, twice the same; retention; client B never reads or writes client
A's figures; through the API, 204 for everything and nothing stored for a client without the pack;
the overview in the portal with no forbidden field.

**Done when** a week of mezonas.com's figures, in the Mezonas client, sits within a sensible band
of Cloudflare's own analytics, and no IP address is in the database, Workers Logs or Sentry.

## 5. Things to verify, not assume

- Intuit API details (M9) and Google's Gmail and verification rules (M10), see those milestones.
- Anthropic: PDF input limits, the tool-use output format with `tool_choice`, and current model prices (M2/M4). Model ids come from agent definitions, with defaults in §2 of the architecture.
- Cloudflare Workflows (the deploy spike): `waitForEvent` event-type naming rules and maximum timeout (the 5-day doc request must fit); whether an event sent just before the wait starts is kept or lost (the check-before-wait in §1.2 covers the common case); `create` refusing a used instance id; step result size and step count limits; testing waits in `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers`. Plenary Labs uses `step.do` and `step.sleep` but not `waitForEvent`, so this part is new.
- Neon + Hyperdrive: `cs_worker` created with SQL on Neon, `force row level security`, and that Hyperdrive's transaction pooling keeps `set_config(..., true)` inside the transaction (session-level settings would leak between requests).
- A Workflow step making a long Claude call (30–90 s) completes. Workers limit CPU time, not time spent waiting on I/O (Plenary Labs' ADR 0001 relies on this too).

## 6. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Extraction accuracy on messy scans | Totals validation routes failures to review; re-extraction inserts new rows; goldens catch regressions. |
| Matcher false positives (the costly error) | Hard-rule veto on key attributes; precision gate in CI; auto-threshold set per agent version. |
| The builder grows into a project of its own | Scope is fixed by ARCHITECTURE §10.5 and the owner's design; a structured list is far simpler than a node canvas; forms and the palette come from schemas, not hand-built per skill; Pia's workflows are seeded as data, so M8 doesn't wait on builder polish. |
| An automatic write does something unwanted in a client's system | Writes default to approval and delete is off; only the operator loosens a matrix and the client can tighten it at any time; every write records its before state and the run that made it; actions show in the run view and the portal timeline. |
| Generic platform, nothing finished | Every milestone ends with something runnable; M8 (Pia) is the proof that the platform is enough to build a real agent; nothing in `packages/*` may special-case a pack (layering check). |
| A write to a client's system goes wrong | Every external write is approved by a person, idempotent, records the prior state, and fails on a stale `SyncToken`; test runs never write. |
| RLS bypassed by connecting as owner | The Worker only has `cs_worker`; a test asserts `current_user` isn't the owner in the request path. |
| Workflows' wait semantics differ from this plan | Checked in the M0 deploy spike, before the executor is built on them. The executor only sees `StepRunner`, so a change stays in `apps/api`. |
| QBO app approval delays | Upload connector is the demo path; M9 runs only against the sandbox. |
| Google verification for Gmail's restricted scopes takes weeks, or testing-mode tokens expire after 7 days | Start the Google app verification as soon as M10 begins, in parallel with the build; for Workspace clients, domain-wide delegation granted by their admin avoids it; upload and QBO attachments cover the demo meanwhile. |
| A malicious email steers an agent | Email text only ever reaches a model inside tool results; Gmail writes are drafts and labels, each approved; ingestion is limited to the connection's query. |
| The operator becomes the bottleneck | Templates mean a client agent starts from proven work; template updates reach every linked client as one review each; the back office ranks what needs attention across clients; the Builder drafts from requests. |
| A bad version reaches every client | Template publishes change no client; each client's update is a reviewed merge with a test run on that client; rollback is per client. |
| Merges lose or garble a client's customizations | Merge is pure, deterministic and covered by golden cases; conflicts are never auto-resolved; the merged workflows must validate; the merge draft is test-run before publish; the previous version stays one click away. |
| Forks drift until templates stop helping | The template page shows customizations and versions behind for every client; promotion folds good client changes back; detaching is explicit. |
| Client data or learning leaks across clients | RLS on `app.tenant_ids` with tests for client and operator sessions; memory and learned records are per client; runs set exactly one client. |

## 7. Owner answers and open questions

Answered (2026-11-03)
- Name and design: the product is **Mezonas**, on `mezonas.com` (already in Cloudflare), with the Mezonas design system; `countersign` stays the code name. Public cloud resources are named Mezonas; app on `app.mezonas.com`, staging on `staging.mezonas.com` (confirmed).
- Hosting: keep Cloudflare + Neon for consistency with Plenary Labs (§1.1). Sign-in: WorkOS, as in Plenary Labs.
- UI: the owner's v1 screens are in `docs/design/`, a starting point to rework page by page (2026-11-03).
- Connectors work at the client portal level: the operator builds and offers them, clients sign in to their own tools (2026-11-03).
- Approvals are on by default and set per step where a step changes a client's tool (2026-11-03).
- No Slack yet; build an MVP first (2026-11-03).
- Invoices: build on synthetic invoices; real ones can be added later.
- Purchaser routing: undecided. Send all doc requests to an admin for now (§1.1).
- Stack: match Plenary Labs fully: Vite + React + Hono instead of Next.js, Cloudflare Workflows instead of Inngest (ARCHITECTURE v0.2).

- Product shape: an agent platform (connections, skills, agents, drag-and-drop workflows, write-back); material cost management is the first agent, as a pack (ARCHITECTURE v0.3).
- Managed service: advisors review, build and publish; clients connect, approve and request changes (ARCHITECTURE v0.4).
- Managed-agents service suite: the operator builds and runs agents from a back office; clients get a portal for reports, runs, approvals, connections and requests (ARCHITECTURE v0.5).
- Templates and client agents: agents are built per client from reusable templates, versioned per client with lineage, template updates merged per client, client changes promotable to templates (ARCHITECTURE v0.6).
- Provisioning: GitHub Actions bootstrap (option 1), Neon in the same region as Plenary Labs.
- Separation: Mezonas is fully separate from Plenary Labs (no runtime dependency, data, infrastructure, keys or secrets in common); it follows Plenary Labs' stack, rules and patterns for consistency (owner, 2026-11-03).
- Connectors: QuickBooks Online and Gmail first, plus upload (ARCHITECTURE v0.4).
- `main` created from the setup branch (2026-11-03).

Answered (2026-11-04, ARCHITECTURE v0.9)
1. Email for the MVP: **Gmail goes into the MVP.** Start Google's OAuth app verification now and see how long it takes (it was quick on another project). Forwarding stays as built.
2. QuickBooks: **reads and the planned writes**: `qbo.attachable.upload`, `qbo.bill.update_lines` / `qbo.purchase.update_lines` (total unchanged), `update_memo`, `update_purchase_cost`; each behind approval, delete off.
3. The millwork shop's mailbox: **not part of this plan.** Pia and the millwork use case are built separately; the Materials pack (M8) stays in its own branches and is not merged here.
4. Approvals: **approver rules decide who approves** (§1.17).
5. Billing: **Stripe**, a monthly base fee per client plus metered usage from `usage_daily` (§1.17).

Answered (2026-11-06, ARCHITECTURE v0.12; the lead's recommended defaults, recorded as decisions; the owner may revisit)
1. **Gmail stays send-only, with no CASA.** Reading mail is forwarding: the client sets a Gmail filter that forwards a label to its Mezonas address. "Write in your voice" learns only from **voice samples** the client pastes or forwards into the knowledge base (`voice_sample`), never from reading the mailbox (ARCHITECTURE §6.4, §1.42).
2. **QuickBooks: create bill and accounts receivable.** `qbo.bill.create` behind approval (never automatic by default); sync of Invoice, Customer and Payment; `qbo.aging_report`; `qbo.invoice.add_note`, which only updates PrivateNote. Delete stays off (ARCHITECTURE §6.2, §1.41).
3. **Billing stays Stripe invoices paid by link.** No card on file or automatic charge in v0.12; listed as future (ARCHITECTURE §1 "Out").
4. **Signing is never done by text reply.** A text or email links to the Desk; signing needs a signed-in session (ARCHITECTURE §11.1, §11.3).
5. **Go-live stages:** test bench (Sandbox or test runs, never writing); trial on client data (every write needs approval whatever the step says, about 14 days or 25 runs, whichever comes later, operator-adjustable); client sign-off (the client admin approves going live); live. Recorded per client agent; live is blocked until the earlier stages pass; the operator can skip a stage with a logged reason (ARCHITECTURE §10.9, §1.36).

Answered (2026-11-06, ARCHITECTURE v0.13, owner)
- **The agent oversees its runs.** The workflow stays the operator's plan; clean steps run as code,
  `task` skills (typed jobs, the same in any agent) on their own default model, usually cheap, and
  `agent` skills (judgment and drafting in the agent's voice) on the agent's model with its
  instructions and memory; a step may override either's model; the agent
  reviews failed steps (and, where set, steps that feed a write) on a model the operator picks, and
  fixes what it can within the plan. Lasting fixes are proposals. Build order: a model per step (M30),
  oversight on failures (M31), checkpoint reviews (M32).
- **No graph library.** We keep our executor on Cloudflare Workflows rather than LangGraph (ARCHITECTURE
  §10.11).

Open
1. Stripe: the base fee is per active agent, with a credit allowance and overage (owner, 2026-11-04). One credit is one cent of our cost (owner, 2026-11-04). Open: the allowance per agent, whether a client's allowances are pooled across its agents, the prices, and the Stripe account and its keys (saved as network secrets, never in the repo).
2. Google: the Cloud project and OAuth client for Gmail (`GOOGLE_*`), and starting the verification (owner: tomorrow).
3. Intuit: a QuickBooks developer app (`QBO_*`), and `developer.intuit.com` allowed in the session's network so M9 can check the docs first (owner: tomorrow).

Remove `gmail.readonly`, `gmail.labels` and `gmail.compose` from the Google consent screen. Verification waits for the public site.

## 8. Execution plan, 2026-11-06 night

Autonomous build of ARCHITECTURE v0.12 by several build agents, coordinated by the lead session. Each
stream has a decisions section (§1.36–§1.52) and a milestone (M13–M29). v0.13 adds two Wave 2
streams, W2-H and W2-I (§1.53–§1.54, M30–M32).

### 8.1 Autonomy rules

- Branch from `origin/main`: `claude/<stream id lower case>-<short name>` (e.g. `claude/w1a-golive`).
  Open the feature PR as a **draft** at once; mark it ready when `pnpm check` passes locally and the
  milestone's done-when holds in tests.
- **Migrations.** Wave 1's migrations 0018–0025 are written by **one agent in one PR**
  (`claude/w1-migrations`, from ARCHITECTURE §5.5 as written), so the Drizzle snapshots chain; it
  merges first, and every Wave 1 stream builds on it (merge main once it lands). From Wave 2 each stream
  with a migration opens its own migration PR before its feature PR (CLAUDE.md). Migrations are
  additive only and keep the previous Worker working (they apply before the new Worker deploys).
  Never edit a migration that has reached `main`. If merge order differs from the numbers, the lead
  renumbers before merging (file, journal tag, increasing `when`), never after.
- The lead reviews and merges on green CI, migration PRs first; merge commits only. `main` deploys to
  staging. **Never** production, a `v*` tag, or anything of Plenary Labs.
- After two CI failures with the same cause, stop and report to the lead with the cause.
- **UI:** only where an existing screen would break or a check needs it; the owner's redesign replaces
  the rest. Keep today's screens working.
- **No new third-party accounts or keys.** Build against fakes (`@countersign/connectors/testing`), record
  what is unchecked in the connector's `NOTES.md`. Tests never call the live model (`LLM_MODE=replay`
  or a FakeModel).
- **Merge `origin/main` often:** at least before opening ready, and whenever another stream merges.

### 8.2 Shared hot spots

Rule: **append-only edits in clearly separated blocks**, each headed `// v0.12 <stream id>: …`; never
reorder, reformat or rewrite another stream's block; on conflict keep both blocks.

|---|---|
| `packages/core/src/api/routes.ts` | One import and one `...xRoutes` line per stream; route specs live in the stream's own `packages/core/src/api/<area>.ts` |

### 8.5 Stream briefs

Each brief stands alone. Read CLAUDE.md, ARCHITECTURE v0.12 (the sections named) and the decisions
section first; the files listed under "Owns" in §8.4 are yours; touch hot spots only as §8.2 says.

**W1-M: Wave 1 migrations (0018–0025).** One branch `claude/w1-migrations`, one PR. Write the tables and
columns of ARCHITECTURE §5.5 for 0018–0025 exactly, in `packages/db/src/schema.ts` and as migrations
generated in order (`drizzle-kit generate`, then hand-written SQL for RLS, grants, checks, triggers
and the 0019 backfill). Forced RLS on every new tenant table; grants per §5.5 (insert-only where it
says so; `thread_read` may update; nothing gets DELETE). The `knowledge_entry` kind check is replaced,
not dropped. Tests: migrations apply on an empty database and on one with the seed; RLS loop covers
the new tables; the 0019 backfill sets live agents to `live`. Done when CI is green and the previous
Worker's tests still pass against the new schema.

**W1-A: Go-live stages and client controls (M13, §1.36).** ARCHITECTURE §10.9, §11.5. Build stages
and transitions in core (`lifecycle/`), `effectiveMode` and its three call sites, the hourly trial →
signoff pass with the client admin's go-live item (words in six languages), skips with reasons, client
pause/pause all/resume with `until` (minute cron), Run now (`client.canRun` on manual triggers, forms,
server-side validation), and `POST /api/ops/agents/:id/run-event` for the first-invoice gap (reuse the
router's matching and run ids). Extend the API e2e: activate puts the agent in trial and the first
invoice runs live from the back office. Done when M13's check holds.

**W1-B: Notifications, the Table and the Weekly Table (M14, §1.37).** ARCHITECTURE §11.3, §11.4.
Build the `notification` outbox and its 5-minute pass (scan rows; no hooks in others' code), the
mailer over the `resend` client and texts through the client's Twilio subaccount as release codes do,
per-person preferences, `/remind`, `GET /api/table`, the Weekly Table (Friday draft, lead edits, Monday
7:00 send, PDF with `pdf-lib` pinned). No record content in any message (a test walks them). Decision
replies go into the lead thread once W1-C is merged (501 before). Done when M14's check holds.

**W1-C: Threads, the lead and `/commands` (M15, §1.38).** ARCHITECTURE §11.2. Give `packages/threads`
its store, access rules, read state and command resolution; `parseCommand` in core; thread routes for
both surfaces; the lead setting and its session field; lead checks in `createForPerson`;
`threads.post` and `threads.read`; emit `thread.message_posted` (no text). Starting runs from commands
is W2-E's. Done when M15's check holds.

**W1-D: The Desk, sign-off and viewers (M16, §1.39).** ARCHITECTURE §11.1, §4. Build `explainWrite`
and `whyApproval`, person `choices`, snooze, edits before signing (`editable` display fields, re-check,
re-route), batches (`batch_key`, batch resolve), the undo window (`deliver_after`, delivery from the
cron's resume, the check before waiting, undo route), and viewers (`can_sign`, `mayResolve`, rule
validation, `/api/team`). Write a Workers-pool test for undo. Done when M16's check holds.

**W1-E: Run controls and model calls (M17, §1.40).** ARCHITECTURE §10.10. Build `RunFilter` on both
runs lists, retry from the failed step with carried approvals (original idempotency keys, 72 hours,
gate re-check), cancel, replay as a test run in the same client, error keys and grouping, saved views,
the runs CSV, and model calls (port after each call, row plus redacted body in R2, operator-only
routes). Done when M17's check holds.

**W1-F: QuickBooks AR and create bill (M18, §1.41).** ARCHITECTURE §6.2. Add Customer, Invoice and
Payment to sync and CDC, the `receivable` mapping and events, the four reads (aging report live),
`qbo.invoice.add_note` (PrivateNote only, sparse) and `qbo.bill.create` (duplicate check, `requestid`
from the key, `amount`), all in the fake. Write unchecked Intuit facts into `qbo/NOTES.md`. Done when
M18's check holds.

**W1-G: People and voice samples (M19, §1.42).** ARCHITECTURE §6.4, §6.1 (autonomy). Build
`contact.autonomy` with operator-only `auto`, its enforcement in core's reach evaluator and at the gate
(`ToolSpec.recipient`, `decideToolCall`'s `ceilings`), QuickBooks vendor import (customers after
W1-F), voice samples by paste and by the `+voice` forwarding tag, `knowledge.voice_samples` and the
`writing.draft-in-voice` skill. Notes stay data in tool results. Done when M19's check holds.

**W1-H: Platform fixes (M20, §1.43).** ARCHITECTURE §8, §13, §6.2, §14. Dispatch every emitted event
after its transaction commits (requests, background reading, sync steps, run steps, cron passes),
keeping the sweep; record each extraction's tokens and cost and roll background reading into
`usage_platform_daily` and credits; count an agent's documents by what its runs used; add the portal
records list; make "sync now" start or queue. Extend the API e2e: the run starts without the sweep and
usage shows the reading. Done when M20's check holds.

**W2-A: Per-entity permissions and the connectors catalog (M21, §1.44).** ARCHITECTURE §6.1. Add
`permissionEntities`, `ToolSpec.entity`, matrix and grant `entities`, the folded decision with scopes,
entity lines in every manifest, tighten-only per entity, migration 0026 and the catalog routes with
default access for new clients. Old matrices need no migration. Done when M21's check holds.

**W2-B: Automations (M22, §1.45).** ARCHITECTURE §10.7. Migration 0027; the schema, validation,
registry (draft, publish, rollback, shared copies), expansion before execution, approval overrides,
save-as-automation from steps, and a read-only builder card so the old builder doesn't break. Done when
M22's check holds.

**W2-C: Test bench and the agent lab (M23, §1.46).** ARCHITECTURE §9.10. Migration 0028; cases and
matchers, the fixture tool port (no connector, no write), skill and template suite runs (a Workflow for
long suites), metrics, the publish gate, the lab route. Client cases stay in their client. Done when
M23's check holds.

**W2-D: Sloan (M24, §1.47).** ARCHITECTURE §9.8. The supervisor template v2 and its install route, the
account tools over portal-shaped reads, drafted changes applied by the portal's own code, the publish
guard, and a test that walks every tool result for forbidden fields. `pass_to_agent` last, after W2-E.
Done when M24's check holds.

**W2-E: Triggers (M25, §1.48).** ARCHITECTURE §10.1, §10.8. The trigger schema (`moreTriggers`,
`handoff`), validation, the router, `inbound.message` by email (agent addresses) and text, People
filters and polite replies, webhook dedupe and limits, holidays, `run.succeeded` and handoffs, the
triggers list. Done when M25's check holds.

**W2-F: Command center and search (M26, §1.49).** ARCHITECTURE §13.1, §11.6. The command-center route
and feed, search for both surfaces, plain words to `RunFilter` (replay fixtures). Done when M26's
check holds.

**W2-G: KPIs, client view, credits, audit export (M27, §1.50).** ARCHITECTURE §9.9, §11.5, §11.7.
`clientView` and its validation, KPI computation and routes (and use in the Table and the Weekly
Table), credits-out on the hourly billing pass, the client's audit export and a run's audit trail.
Done when M27's check holds.

**W2-H: Task skills and a model per step (M30, §1.53).** ARCHITECTURE §9.2, §10.11 (first part). Add the
`task` mode (own required model; no agent instructions or memory in its prompt), `model` on skill
steps as an override, one resolution function used by `run-agent.ts` and `planned.ts`, validation of
skills and steps with known ids passed in, the Skills library's mode and model, `GET /api/ops/models`, and the run view's per-step model. No migration, no UI
beyond what breaks. Small; start as soon as W1-E merges. Done when M30's check holds.

**W2-I: Run oversight (M31, then M32; §1.54).** ARCHITECTURE §10.11, §5.6. Migration 0029 (the Wave 2
migrations PR) first. Then the decision schema, `oversight` and `review`, `OversightPort` with the executor hook (one
block, after W1-E; agree the order with W2-B, which touches the executor for expansion), `oversee.ts`
on the fake model, the write check before any retry, limits, ask and proposal items, client words in
six languages, the run view and `GET /api/ops/oversight`. M31 (failures) ends green and merges before
M32 (checkpoints) starts. Extend the API e2e: a failing lookup is corrected and the run finishes.
Done when M31's and M32's checks hold.

**W3-A: The Builder as a conversation (M28, §1.51).** ARCHITECTURE §9.6. Migration 0030; sessions,
turns, per-change undo by merge, `/skill`, `/automation`, `/trigger`, similarity, draft-skill with
cases, save as automation. Drafts start with `oversight.review: 'failures'` and may set step models
(v0.13). Done when M28's check holds.

**W3-B: New connectors (M29, §1.52).** ARCHITECTURE §6.11. Google Drive, then Outlook, against fakes;
Ramp and HubSpot after; nothing for the aggregator or Slack until the owner decides. Done when M29's
check holds.

**v0.14 capture: WhatsApp passive capture (M33, §1.55).** ARCHITECTURE §6.12, §5.5. Three PRs, each a
draft from `origin/main`: the design; migration 0031 alone (merged with a merge commit before the
build); the build against the fake Twilio, extended with media. Stay out of the bank-feed connector
and `platform.ts`. Done when M33's check holds.

**Wave 4 (owner, 2026-11-06).** Plan §8 had no Wave 4; the owner added it. Migrations: 0031 belongs to
WhatsApp passive capture (M33, §1.55); 0032 was Wave 4's spare and went to P-1 (2026-11-07: drizzle-kit numbers by journal position, which the journal test checks), so the gchat lock follow-up takes 0033. The bank and card feed (W3-B's row 5,
vendor Plaid, owner 2026-11-06) is being built alongside and is not a Wave 4 stream.

**W4-A: WhatsApp through Meta (§1.19).** The `whatsapp_meta` connector: the client connects its own
WhatsApp Business Account from the portal through Meta's Embedded Signup (the operator never holds its
credentials); the same `whatsapp.*` tool ids as Twilio (`sharedNamespaces`), resolved by the agent's
binding; Meta's webhook verified by `X-Hub-Signature-256` over the raw body; inbound messages once per
`wamid`; media by Meta's two-step download into M33's capture, under each conversation's setting;
groups (`conversation.kind = group`) only where the client has Official Business Account status
(to verify against Meta's docs). Against a fake. Going live needs Mezonas registered as a Meta Tech
Provider (owner). Starts once M33 merges. Done when a Meta-connected client's direct conversation
captures a document exactly as M33's check does on Twilio, and `whatsapp.send` works through either
connector without the workflow changing.

**W4-B: Follow-ups.** Small items the Wave 1–3 streams left open, none needing an owner decision:
an entity set to off is caught at publish, not only at run time (W2-A); PDFs over the model's page
limit are split for extraction (stream D); run questions keep an amount filter (W2-F); a step's real
attempt count (W2-B's clean-run rate); `/remind` for a connection to fix, by email through Resend
(M5); Zoho CRM and Podio sync and webhooks (stream G); directory upkeep through `directory.suggest`
(§1.27). Each item its own commit; record decisions where the item's section is.

**W4-C: Slack (ARCHITECTURE §6.11, row 6).** The `slack` connector: Slack OAuth v2 installing a bot
with `chat:write` (and `channels:read` to list channels); the client admin installs it into the
client's own workspace from the portal and picks the channels an agent may post to;
`slack.message.post` is `external` (create, entity `message`), behind approval, posted once (the
attempt recorded before Slack is called, as `gmail.send`); no reading of messages. Against a fake.
Owner: a Slack app with the redirect URI and public distribution. Done when an approved post goes
once to a picked channel and a channel not picked is refused.

**W4-D: Google Sheets (ARCHITECTURE §6.11, row 7).** The `gsheets` connector on the Google client
Drive and Gmail use, with the `drive.file` scope only (non-sensitive): sheets Mezonas creates, and
sheets the client picks with Google's file picker. Tools: `sheets.range.read` (read),
`sheets.rows.append` (`external`, create) and `sheets.range.update` (`external`, update, the cells'
current values as before state, refused if they changed since). Behind approval, once per action.
Disconnect drops our tokens without revoking (the shared client, as Drive). Against a fake. Owner:
the Sheets API enabled, the picker's browser key restricted to our origins. Done when an agent
appends a row once to a picked sheet after approval, and a sheet the client didn't pick is refused.

**W4-E: Google Chat (ARCHITECTURE §6.11, row 8).** Slack's counterpart for clients on Google
Workspace: a Google Chat app of Mezonas' own that posts to the spaces the client adds it to. The
app authenticates as itself (a service account with the `chat.bot` scope; no per-client user tokens).
A space belongs to a client only after proof: the client admin gets a one-time code in the portal and
mentions the app with it in the space; the app's interaction endpoint (Google-signed bearer token
verified) binds that space to that client. Never by email domain alone. `gchat.message.post` is
`external` (create, entity `message`), behind approval, posted once (a client-assigned message id
where the API takes one, else the attempt recorded first), plain text with mentions of everyone
stripped; only to bound spaces; no reading of messages beyond the binding mention. Removing the app
from a space unbinds it. Against a fake. Owner: the Chat API configured on the Google project (app
name, avatar, HTTP endpoint), the service account and its key as a secret, and the app published to
the Google Workspace Marketplace (each client's Workspace admin must allow it). Done when an approved
post goes once to a bound space, an unbound space is refused, and a space is bound only by its code.
